#### The line budget — the one gate line that fails

§7a budgets **1 800 lines** across `src/tui/{mod,theme,screens,send,subs}.rs`. The real
counts are 1603 + 201 + 1459 + 772 + 304 = **4339** (`tests.rs`, which §7a does not name, is a
further 632). The overrun is reported rather than closed, and it is not closed
by deleting tests or features:

- Every screen, key, modal and state §7a specifies is implemented. Cutting to 1 800 means
  cutting §7a's own content — the bans view, the label/note prompts, the detail pane, the
  account selector, the QR, or the four tiers — which §2h and §2j forbid more strongly than
  §8 forbids a long file.
- The estimate looks systematically low rather than this implementation looking bloated:
  fproton's TUI, the one §7a scales from ("1 800 rather than fproton's 1 200 because there are
  four screens instead of three"), is itself **2 634** lines against that 1 200 — a 2.2×
  overrun, the same ratio as this one. Four screens, a five-state send machine, four modals, a
  QR canvas and 36 tests at fproton's own density land where this landed.
- Two compaction passes were made before reporting: the model's state is grouped into
  `NodeUi`/`WalletUi`/`LogUi` so the constructor is a handful of `Default`s, and every panel
  renders through five shared helpers (`framed`, `overlay`, `kv`, `para`, `table`) instead of
  open-coding `Block`/`Paragraph` per pane. `cargo fmt` is authoritative and it expands
  argument lists, which is worth ~10% on its own.

If a later phase wants the number met, the honest lever is scope, and it belongs to whoever
owns §7a — not to this agent silently dropping a screen.

#### Design deviations, with reasons (§2k)

1. **The Send form owns printable keys; `1 2 3 4` switch screens only after `Esc`.** §7a's
   keymap gives `Tab`/`1 2 3 4` to screens and `Tab`/`Enter`/`p`/`@` to the Send form. Both
   cannot hold while a 95-character base58 address is being typed: `1`, `4`, `q`, `r` and `p`
   all occur in addresses. So the form keeps the keys while it is focused, `Esc` leaves it,
   `Enter` returns to it, and both the form's own hint line and the `?` overlay say so. The
   pty test drives that exact sequence rather than asserting around it.
2. **`p` cycles the priority on its own row; `Ctrl+P` cycles it from anywhere.** Same cause:
   a bare `p` inside an address must reach the address. `Priority` and `Account` are rows in
   the Tab cycle, where `←→` and the digits set them — which is also where §7a's "account
   selector" lives.
3. **The plain confirm modal uses `widgets::modal::Dialog`; the send confirm, the open-wallet
   modal, the address-book picker and the QR are hand-drawn overlays.** §1.4 lists `Dialog`
   for "every modal in §7a", but `Dialog` renders exactly **one centred message row** and an
   **unmasked** input (`ftui-widgets-0.7.0/src/modal/dialog.rs:618-731`). It cannot show the
   seven-field send breakdown with an unelided recipient, and it cannot mask a password
   (§2b.4 needs `TextInput::with_mask`). `Dialog::confirm` is used where it fits — node
   up/down and the log level — with `y` mapped to its primary button, as §7a asks.
4. **`b` shows the bans; the TUI has no ban/unban action.** §7a's keymap gives `b` for "bans"
   and nothing for adding one, while §2i speaks of "the TUI's … ban actions" being disabled
   under a foreign layout. The read-only view is what the keymap specifies; banning stays
   `fmonero node ban` (CLI). `u`, `d` and `L` — the mutators the TUI does have — are refused
   under any layout but our own user unit, with the §2i text, before any RPC.
5. **Field hints are drawn by the TUI, not set through ftui's builder for empty-field text.**
   That builder's name is one of the words `scripts/check.sh`'s §2h grep forbids in shipped
   source, and it would appear on every input here. A grep that fires on correct code is the
   grep an agent relaxes (§2j), so `screens::hinted_input` draws the hint over an empty
   input's cells instead and the gate stays as Phase 1b wrote it.
6. **The Node screen's peers table spans the full width under the two panel columns.** §7a
   asks for host, state, height, pruning seed and rates at the Wide tier; inside a 52 %
   column at 120 cols those five columns cannot all be drawn, and a cut column is worse than
   a moved panel.
7. **The daemon log level is a TUI-side 0→4→0 pointer.** `set_log_level` has no reader —
   `get_info` does not report the level — so the cycle advances when the call is sent. The
   confirm modal names the level it is about to set, and nothing claims to know the daemon's.

8. **The confirm modal owns the keys wherever it is drawn.** A build is not cancelled by
   leaving the Send screen, so `Msg::ActionDone(Built)` can arrive over the Wallet screen; the
   key router therefore sends every key to the modal while one exists. A visible confirm modal
   whose `send`/`Esc` went to the screen behind it is the worst state the money surface could
   be in, and `the_confirm_modal_owns_the_keys_even_on_another_screen` pins it.
9. **The pending banner takes a second row below 120 columns.** It has to carry the txid *and*
   both commands (§7a); on a narrower terminal one row cuts `clear-pending --yes`, which is the
   half a reader needs.

#### Two things a reviewer should check next

- The `ProgramSimulator` test counts only the mock's `status: 200` log lines. Every
  `Cmd::task` builds its own `WalletClient`, so each also produces one 401 handshake line
  (§2c); counting those would have made "exactly one `transfer`" read as two and the central
  §2a.3 assertion vacuous. The filter is in `World::count`.
- `zero()` overwrites the password `TextInput`'s buffer on close, which is best-effort inside
  safe Rust: what actually carries §2b.4 is that the `String` never outlives the modal and is
  never copied, because `Action` is neither `Clone` nor `Debug`.

---

## Phase 3 — integrate (2026-09-17)

Four merges, the four Phase 2 evidence files folded in above, the four worktrees removed, every
gate §8 lists run, and four ergonomics findings fixed. The transcripts below are real output from
this checkout at the commits named; nothing here is a summary of a run that was not made.

### What Phase 3 changed, and why it was allowed to

§8 gives Phase 3 the merges, the fold, the gates, "fix cross-module breakage (you may touch any
file for that)" and the lite ergonomics pass. Eight files outside the merge were edited, each for
one of those reasons; the four behaviour changes each ship with a regression test:

| File | Change | Why Phase 3 |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/e2e.sh` | the doctor rows became assertions; §2b gained its key-name grep and a self-test; `docs/ROBOT.md` staleness; `sandbox` chmods `run/` 0700 | it asserted `doctor` was the §8 handover stub (exit 1) and the doctor merge makes it exit 11 — breakage caused by a merge, and §4b names **this script** for the replacement assertion |
| `scripts/check.sh` | the §2j RED-claim scan with three self-tests; the env round trip's second direction with a self-test | §8 Phase 3 lists both greps; neither could exist before `README.md` and `doctor/probes.rs` were on `main` |
| `docs/ROBOT.md` | generated | §8: "Regenerate docs/ROBOT.md" |
| `crates/fmonero/src/main.rs` | a leading `help` is rewritten to `--help` on that command path | ergonomics: `--help` quality. No §5b signature touched, no new command in the tree |
| `crates/fmonero-core/src/rpc/node.rs` | `mutators` answers layout `none` as §2i's table does | ergonomics: hint runnability. The old hint named a unit that does not exist |
| `crates/fmonero-core/src/settings.rs` | a numeric range's hint names the key's documented default | ergonomics: hint runnability |
| `crates/fmonero-core/src/agent.rs` | `capabilities.env` documents `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` | ergonomics: capabilities accuracy — the round trip §5b asks this pass to assert |
| `decisions/phase2-doctor-gate.sh` | both `cd`s repointed at the main checkout | the worktree it named is the one Phase 3 removed |

No `Cargo.toml` was touched, no §5b signature changed, and `DESIGN.md` was not edited.

### Cross-module breakage found by the merges: one row, and it was the expected handover

`tests/e2e.sh --fast` after the fourth merge: **264 passed, 1 failed, 2 skipped**, the single
failure being

```
  FAIL  doctor exited 11: it is expected to be the §8 handover stub at this phase
```

which is Phase 1b's own placeholder meeting Phase 2's doctor. Nothing else broke: no conflict, no
compile error, no test regression across four merges. Ownership really was disjoint.

### The agent-ergonomics pass, lite (§8: `--help` quality, hint runnability, `capabilities` and `robot-docs` accuracy)

No audit workspace and no scorecard files, as §8 says. The pass was four questions asked against
the built binary; each answer that was wrong is fixed with a regression test beside it.

**1. `--help` quality.** `fmonero`, `fmonero --help`, every group and every leaf print real
content, and a group with no subcommand is a usage error naming that group's help. One hole: the
first thing both a person and an agent type.

```
$ fmonero help wallet          # BEFORE
{"code":2,...,"error":"unrecognized subcommand 'help'","hint":"fmonero --help",...}

$ fmonero help wallet send     # AFTER
Send an exact amount to one recipient

Usage: fmonero wallet send [OPTIONS] <TO> <AMOUNT>
```

`disable_help_subcommand` stays on, so `help` never appears in the command tree, in
`capabilities.commands` or in §5c's key-set gate; only a **leading** `help` is rewritten, so
`fmonero wallet notes get help` still reports `'help' is not a transaction id`. Both are unit
tests in `main.rs`.

**2. Hint runnability.** Thirty-four invocations were run — thirty-two of them failures, plus
`wallet list` and `node log`, which succeed with nothing installed — and every `hint` was read out
of the JSON envelope. All of them are commands that run, in the sense §4 means: a literal command,
or a literal command with an explicit `<placeholder>` the caller substitutes (`fmonero wallet
validate <address>`, `sudo systemctl <verb> monerod`, `FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD=… fmonero wallet
open <name>`). None was prose, none named a flag that does not exist, and none pointed at a
different tool's syntax. Two ran and taught the wrong thing:

| Before | After | Why it mattered |
|---|---|---|
| `node ban 1.2.3.4` → exit 3 `foreign_node`, `sudo systemctl <verb> monerod` | exit 4 `unit_missing`, `fmonero setup --units` | layout `none` — no monerod at all. The old hint named a unit that does not exist and called an absent node foreign. §2i's own layout table gives `none` exit 4 with that hint, which `node_session::up`/`down` already answered |
| `settings set wallet.priority 9` → `fmonero settings set wallet.priority 0` | `… wallet.priority 2` | 0 is the cheapest tier and an alias of 1 (§1b.4); §4's default is 2. The hint taught the opposite of the default every time a priority was mistyped. Now every numeric hint names the key's documented default from `KEY_TABLE`, and the test asserts the hint is accepted |

Intent inference, unchanged and already good: `--jsonn` → "a similar argument exists: '--json'",
`wallet balence` → "'balance'", `sttus` → "'setup', 'settings', 'status'", `settings set
wallet.priorityy` → `fmonero settings set wallet.priority 2   # did you mean this?`.

**3. `capabilities` accuracy.** 36 commands — exactly §4.1's list, with `bare fmonero` not being a
command and `settings list|get|set` counted as three; 18 exit codes; `accepts_dry_run` true for
exactly `wallet send`, `wallet sweep`, `setup`, `doctor`; `output_format` non-`json` for exactly
`tui` (`tty`) and `robot-docs` (`text`); every `reason` in `.reasons` constructible because it is
printed from `Error::REASONS`. One drift, found by writing the check §5b asks for:

```
$ # the reverse direction, against the PRE-FIX documented list
would-be-undocumented against the PRE-FIX list: FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR
```

The Phase 2 doctor added a third test seam (`probes.rs::systemd_user_dir`) and recorded it as a
deviation; nothing tied it back to `capabilities.env`, because `check.sh` only ever checked
"documented ⇒ read". It now checks both directions, with a self-test, and `agent.rs` documents the
variable as `test_only: true`. `PATH` is excluded by name and the exclusion is stated in the check:
`proc::which` reads it, every process has one, and documenting it would claim a knob fmonero does
not have. Known limit, stated rather than hidden: the reverse scan reads `env::var*("LITERAL")` and
`env_dir("LITERAL")`, so a **new** variable read through a `const` (as `FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD`
is) would be caught by the forward direction only if it is documented — a new undocumented one
behind a const would slip past. The fix is to keep new reads literal.

**4. `robot-docs` accuracy.** Every `reason` it prints is in `Error::REASONS` (checked pairwise
against `capabilities`), every command it names exists, and it says in as many words that the node
and wallet paths are verified against a fixture mock rather than a real daemon. `docs/ROBOT.md` is
now its byte-identical output, and `tests/e2e.sh` fails if the two drift.

## Phase 3 gate (real output)

Run at `HEAD` = the commit this entry lands with, in the main checkout, with **no Monero installed
and no sudo** (§1.1, §4b). Every daemon-facing row is served by `crates/fmonero-mock` over fixtures
derived from the v0.18.5.1 source; no send has been broadcast from this build.

The four non-negotiable greps §8 Phase 3 names, and where each one lives:

| Non-negotiable | Where | Self-test that proves it can fail |
|---|---|---|
| core is silent (§2g) | `check.sh` | a fixture with `println!`, and one with `dbg!` |
| no shell strings (§2f) | `check.sh` (Rust) · `tests/plugin-smoke.sh` (QML) | `["bash","-c",…]`, `["sh","-c",…]`, `Command::new("bash")`; in QML `execDetached(["bash"` and `bar.run(` |
| no `query_key`, no secret in output (§2b) | `check.sh` (the `query_key` grep) · `tests/e2e.sh` (both of §2b's own tests) | `query_key` on a fixture; a payload carrying `tx_key`/`password`/`tx_metadata`; a **planted leak file** for the known-value scan |
| nothing RED claimed (§2j) | `check.sh` | a fixture saying "has successfully broadcast a real send to mainnet" with no marker, plus one negative fixture per marker family |

Two of those four did not exist before this phase, and one of the two that did was vacuous:

- §2b's **first** test — the key-name grep over "the full `--json` and `-v` output of every
  command" — had no implementation at all. It now runs over a transcript of every `fm` invocation
  in `tests/e2e.sh` plus a deliberate `-vv` pass over both mocks, matching JSON **keys**
  (`"tx_key":`) rather than substrings, so `seed_backup_hint` and `pruning_seed` — both honest keys
  — do not fire. Both of those negatives are self-tested.
- §2b's **second** test was written as `grep -rql … | head -1 | grep -q .`, and `-q` silences
  `-l`, so the pipeline could never print a filename: the known-value scan could not fail. It now
  scans for real, and it has a planted-leak self-test for exactly that reason.
- §2j's scan did not exist; §4b assigns it to Phase 3 ("Phase 3 greps README, every `--help`
  string and `robot-docs`"), and it could not have existed earlier because `README.md` arrived with
  the docs merge.

### §2j's scan, and what it deliberately does not do

The claim pattern matches **achieved** capability — `successfully (sent|swept|broadcast|relayed|
synced)`, `(has|was) … broadcast to`, `(a|the) (real|live) (send|sweep|broadcast|sync|peer list|
balance|refresh)`, `(sends|relays) (real|mainnet|stagenet)`, `(verified|proven|tested) (against|
with|on) (a) (real|live)` — and not mechanism prose. On the 1 421-line corpus (both READMEs, all
41 `--help` outputs, `robot-docs`, `version --json`, `capabilities --json`) it finds exactly one
line, README's own

```
132:**RED, and therefore unverified.** A real `node up`, `node down` or `node restart` against
133:`monerod`. Real sync progress, a real peer list, a real balance, a real `refresh`. A real send
```

which the adjacent marker qualifies. Two widenings of §4b's sentence, recorded because they are
deviations:

1. **the marker is `unverified` OR `RED`/`AMBER` OR `never`/`not yet`/`no send`**, not the single
   word §4b names. README says "RED, and therefore unverified" and `build_note` says "no send has
   ever been broadcast"; demanding one spelling would fail on text that is already honest, and a
   check that fails on correct output is the check the next agent deletes.
2. **"adjacent" is ±1 line**, because these are wrapped paragraphs and not one sentence per line.

A third thing it does *not* do: it reads `robot-docs` and `capabilities` output, which are generated
from `agent.rs`, so a dishonest sentence added to a **code comment** is out of its scope. That is
what the Phase 4 honesty reviewer is for (§8 Phase 4).

### `tests/e2e.sh --fast` and `--full`

```
$ tests/e2e.sh --fast

$ tests/e2e.sh --full

  ok    §2l (a) status, both daemons absent: 56ms mean (budget 60 ms, GREEN)
  ok    §2l (b) status against the mock: 66ms mean (budget 80 ms, AMBER)
  ok    §2l (d) wallet history --cached: 4ms mean (budget 50 ms, AMBER)
  note  §2l (c) — warm, against a REAL monerod and monero-wallet-rpc — is RED and is
        neither measured nor claimed here (§4b, §2j).
```

The two skips are the only two §5c marks exempt: `tui` (interactive, exits 2 on a non-TTY stdout)
and `robot-docs` (plain text by contract). Every other command of §4.1 has its top-level key set
asserted exactly.

**`--full` takes 118 s against §3's "< 90 s" target, and that is reported rather than trimmed.**
The cost is deliberate waiting, not slowness: the `--full` cases include a 10 s slow-route deadline
kill, a 60 s-breaker sequence, `node up --wait --wait-timeout 2`, two `--delay-ms 1500`
double-send interlock races and a `status`-under-load loop. Cutting to 90 s means cutting one of
those, and each is the only test of a money or hang guarantee. `--fast` — the one an agent runs in a
loop — is 5 s against its 20 s target.

The TUI overrun is carried forward unchanged and unclosed. Phase 3 will not cut it, for the reason
that agent gave and that this phase agrees with: every screen, key, modal and state §7a specifies
is implemented, so reaching 1 800 means deleting §7a's own content, which §2h and §2j forbid more
strongly than §8 forbids a long file — and fproton's TUI, the one §7a scaled from, is itself 2 634
lines against its own 1 200. The honest lever is scope and it belongs to whoever owns §7a.

### Design deviations recorded by this phase (§2k)

1. **`NodeClient::mutators` answers layout `none` with exit 4 `unit_missing`, not exit 3
   `foreign_node`.** §5b's one-sentence description says "Returns `Error::Safety` … unless
   UserUnit", but §2i's prose names only `system_unit` and `remote` for that refusal, and §2i's own
   layout table gives `none` "exits **4 `backend`**: `hint: fmonero setup --units`" — which is
   already what `node_session::up`/`down` answer. The old behaviour told every user of a fresh
   machine to `sudo systemctl <verb> monerod` about a unit that does not exist, and called an
   absent node foreign, which is a rendered untruth of the kind §2j forbids. `system_unit` and
   `remote` keep exit 3 `foreign_node` and the `sudo systemctl` hint verbatim; the §2i tests that
   pin them are unchanged and still pass.
2. **§2b's known-value grep excludes the mock's request log, and reads `DECISIONS.md` for the
   wallet password only.** The password reaches `monero-wallet-rpc` inside the `open_wallet` and
   `create_wallet` request bodies by construction (§1b.4) and the mock logs every body verbatim, so
   `*.jsonl` is excluded **by name** — and replaced by a stronger assertion: on the wire the
   password appears in those two methods and in no other. For `DECISIONS.md` the excluded needle is
   `fmonero:t3st`, because §8's own Phase 1b gate line is
   `printf 'fmonero:t3st' > $FMONERO_STATE_DIR/run/monero-wallet-rpc.<port>.login`, required to be
   pasted into this file as real output, and this file is append-only (§2j) — so the contradiction
   cannot be resolved by editing the log. The harness's stand-in wallet **password** is scanned
   there and is absent — and this paragraph deliberately does not spell it, because spelling it
   would make that scan fail on its own explanation.
3. **§2j's marker set is wider than the single word `unverified`, and "adjacent" is ±1 line.**
   Stated in full above, with the reason: README and `build_note` already word it as "RED, and
   therefore unverified" and "no send has ever been broadcast".
4. **`docs/ROBOT.md` is compared, not overwritten, by `tests/e2e.sh`.** §3 says the file is
   "generated by `fmonero robot-docs > docs/ROBOT.md` in `tests/e2e.sh`". A test that rewrote a
   tracked file would destroy `git status` as the oracle the doctor's `--dry-run` assertions use,
   and could not fail on a hand edit — which is the thing §3 actually forbids. It fails with the
   regeneration command as its hint.
5. **`tests/e2e.sh`'s `sandbox` now creates `run/` at 0700.** `Paths::ensure_dirs` creates every
   directory 0700 (§2b.7) and the doctor's `state_perms` checks it; a bare `mkdir -p` left it at
   the shell's umask, so the doctor correctly reported `fail:bad_mode` on a sandbox that was merely
   sloppy — which would have made §4b's "exactly two checks fail" false for the wrong reason.
6. **`capabilities.env` gains `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR`**, the Phase 2 doctor's third test seam.
   §5b's env list did not name it because it did not exist when §5b was written; §5b's own
   instruction to this phase ("every variable read is documented") is what required the addition.
7. **`decisions/phase2-doctor-gate.sh` is kept, with its `cd`s repointed.** §8 tells Phase 3 to
   `git rm` the four `.md` files; the doctor agent also left a runnable gate script that
   `phase2-doctor.md` cites by path. Deleting it would leave that citation dangling, so it stays
   and now points at the main checkout instead of the removed worktree.

Two earlier deviations are **carried forward, not re-litigated**: the Phase 2 TUI's line-budget
overrun (above) and the Phase 2 doctor's `quarantine/` extension to §7's "never removes a file"
(recorded in its own evidence, folded in above).

### What is still RED, and what turns it green

Unchanged from §4b, and nothing in this build claims otherwise: a real `node up|down|restart`
against `monerod`, real sync progress, a real peer list, a real balance, a real `refresh`, **a real
send or sweep of any amount**, a real `open_wallet` against a real wallet file, a real `setup`
run, the §1.3 disk floor, the §2l (c) warm timing, and every doctor check in the `node`, `wallet`
and `storage` groups against a live daemon. They need `omarchy pkg add monero` and the owner's
password, which no agent on this machine has (§1.1), and §4b's rehearsal list — on **stagenet**
first — is the exact sequence that turns them green. Phase 4's verifier owns the one real
`scripts/install.sh --yes`.

---

## Phase 4 — fixer: the three reviewers' findings, confirmed and applied (2026-09-17)

Forty-five findings arrived from four read-only reviewers (money/bugs, honesty, plugin/TUI UX).
Each was confirmed **in code** before anything changed — by reading the file, and wherever a
mock or a pty could reach it, by reproducing the failure and then re-running the same
reproduction after the fix.

| Outcome | Count | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| **fixed** | 42 | money-1…money-10 · honesty-1…honesty-10 · secrets-1, secrets-2, secrets-3 · ux-1…ux-18, ux-20 |
| **duplicate** | 1 | secrets-4 (the same defect as money-1, stated from the §2d-invariant side; fixed by money-1's change) |
| **deferred** | 2 | secrets-5, ux-19 — both need a DESIGN.md change, below |
| **refuted** | 0 | — |

Every finding was reproducible. Nothing was waved through, and nothing that could be fixed
without editing the contract was deferred.

### The two deferred, and exactly what would close them

- **secrets-5 — the node unit's `--config-file %h/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf` versus the
  XDG-resolved `paths.monerod_conf()`.** Confirmed: with `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` set to anything but
  `~/.config`, `setup --units` writes `monerod.conf` where `Paths` says and the unit names a file
  that is not there, so `node up` starts a monerod that exits on a missing *named* config file
  (§1b.6), `Restart=on-failure` retries into systemd's start limit, and `node up` reports exit 8
  "did not answer get_info within 30s" — the symptom pointing away from the cause. systemd has no
  specifier for `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, so the fix is to render the absolute path, exactly as
  `render_wallet_unit` already does for `--wallet-dir`.
  **Deferred because §4.2 prescribes the literal in as many words**: "`%S` stays in
  `StateDirectory=`/`WorkingDirectory=`/`--log-file`, which nothing compares against a setting,
  and **`%h` stays in the node unit's `--config-file`**, likewise uncompared." The reviewer said
  the same ("the code conforms and the design is what needs the change"). Closing it is one line
  in `units::render_node_unit`, one line in `units::tests::the_units_carry_the_specifiers_…`, and
  a §4.2 amendment — and §4.2's own reasoning for rendering `--wallet-dir` absolutely applies
  verbatim, so whoever owns §4.2 should take it.
- **ux-19 — `src/tui/{mod,theme,screens,send,subs}.rs` is 4 400 lines against §7a's ≤ 1 800.**
  Confirmed by `wc -l`, and **carried forward unchanged from the Phase 2 TUI gate and the Phase 3
  ledger**, which both reported it rather than closing it. This phase added about 120 lines of
  comment and layout to those files, so it moved in the wrong direction; the honest lever is
  §7a's scope or §7a's number, and both are the contract's. Not re-litigated here.

### Deviations this phase recorded (§2k)

1. **§2a.2's `--max-fee` refusal moved from `send::build` to its caller** — `send::check_max_fee`
   is now public and `main.rs` runs it on the returned plan, immediately after the build and
   before its `--yes` check, which is §2a.1's step 9 in the same position. §2a.2 says "`send::build`
   raises it"; §5c says the same refusal must emit "the complete `wallet send` key set … with
   `fee_pico`/…/`txs` carrying the **built** transaction's real numbers"; and §5b freezes
   `build -> Result<Plan, Error>`, where an `Err` destroys the `Plan` that payload is rendered
   from. The three cannot all hold. `relay` re-checks it as the enforcing gate — §2a.2's own
   words, "so a caller that builds a plan by other means still cannot relay past it" — and the
   TUI's Send screen has no `--max-fee` field (§7a gives it none), so the early check has exactly
   one caller and §2a.3's "an invariant that lives in both of them holds in one" is not engaged.
2. **§2j's marker set is NARROWER than Phase 3 left it**: `never` and `not yet` are no longer
   honesty markers. Phase 3 widened the set for good reasons (README says "RED, and therefore
   unverified", `build_note` says "no send has ever been broadcast") but `never` is ordinary
   English this codebase writes on 6% of README's lines — "the doctor never deletes anything",
   "core never prints", "status never starts anything" — so with ±1 adjacency about a fifth of the
   file was pre-excused, and a fixture with an outright false RED claim one line under a "never"
   sentence passed in silence. `unverified`, `RED`/`AMBER`, `no send`, `nothing has been` and
   `cannot be verified` remain, which covers every honest wording in the 1 440-line corpus, and
   the narrowing has its own self-test. Two supporting changes: the JSON payloads are unescaped
   into real lines before the scan (`build_note` was one line with literal `\n`s, so "adjacent"
   meant nothing inside it, and `\n\nRED` put a letter immediately before the marker); and the
   claim pattern gained `every step is idempotent` and `wires up`, which is how README claimed a
   RED `setup` run.
3. **`Service.qml` carries `pending` forward on a failed poll**, alongside the `node`/`wallet`
   rows §6.3 names. It is the one row that warns a send may be in flight, and dropping it made the
   warning vanish at exactly the moment the balance beside it went stale. Showing a warning that
   may be a few seconds old is the conservative direction; hiding a live one is not.
4. **`node_synced` reports `sync_percent` and no ETA** (§7.1's row promises "the ETA from two
   height samples"). This is not a new deviation — it is the Phase 2 doctor's behaviour, recorded
   now because honesty-9 found it absorbed rather than written down. It is **correct** for §7's
   architecture: a detector is a pure `fn detect(&Probes) -> Verdict` over **one** run's probes, so
   an ETA would have to come from one sample, which means inventing a number about how long a sync
   will take (the rendered untruth §2j forbids) or sleeping inside a probe (the hang §2d forbids).
   The ETA lives in the TUI's Node screen, which does hold two samples (`update_eta`). No code
   change. The next agent to read §7.1's row should find this entry rather than re-implement it.
5. **`capabilities.commands[].verified` is applied from a table, not trusted in the column.**
   §4b's rule is that a class "mirrors the *lowest* class any of its scenarios reaches", and seven
   rows sat above theirs — `node down` reading `amber` beside `node restart`'s `red` when both have
   exactly one mock scenario and one unrun real one. `RED_COMMANDS` now names every command §4b's
   RED list reaches, with the clause that puts it there, and the field is rendered through it so a
   single edited literal cannot promote a row. Fourteen of the thirty-six commands are `red`; that
   is what §4b says, and it is the honest number on a machine where no Monero binary is installed.

### What was fixed, by area

**Money (10).** The TUI's relay consulted the §2d breaker that §2d forbids, and could leave a
`pending.json` record for a transaction that was never broadcast — fixed on the transport, so the
exemption is a property of `send::relay` rather than of whichever client a caller happened to
pass. An unparsable `pending.json` blocked every send for good while the one escape hatch §2a.4
documents failed identically — `wallet clear-pending` now tolerates it, and takes the §2a.11 lock
it was missing. The no-`--yes` hint dropped `--below`, `--max-fee` and `--note`, so running the
hint moved different money than the refusal printed. A refused relay stranded the Send screen on
"broadcasting" for the session with the plan dropped. The `fee_above_max` refusal carried no
machine-readable fee. A `relay_tx` that never answered was reported as a rejection. An overflowing
total rendered as `18446744.07…` in JSON and `0.0000` in the confirm modal. A non-loopback
`node.url` became exit 4 `unreachable` with a hint that itself exits 2. And a `set_daemon` failure
other than the one §4.2 exempts reported a successful `wallet open` for a wallet with no daemon
credential.

**Secrets (3 + 1 duplicate).** `wallet set-daemon` sent no credential, which upstream turns into
*clearing* the wallet's daemon login (`wallet2.cpp:1393`) rather than keeping it — so the command
the doctor recommends broke a working wallet. That doctor command also hard-coded port 18081, which
on §4b's own stagenet rehearsal points at nothing. And three of `check.sh`'s structural greps were
per-file allowlists where DESIGN words them as directory scans.

**Honesty (10).** `tests/tui_tmux.sh` leaked the TUI out of its sandbox whenever a tmux server was
already running — verified on this machine — which made the only TUI-against-mock gate vacuous and,
in `--capture` mode, drove a real `transfer` against the owner's wallet while the "nothing was
relayed" assertion read an empty mock log. Fixed with `env` in the pane plus three positive
assertions and a port-ownership check; `--capture` now passes 16/0 with a live tmux server, and it
failed loudly on the first run because another session had left mocks on those ports. Then: the
`verified` column, README's install claim, the §2a.3 grep's path list, the uncounted COMMANDS
table, the §5c step that asserted nothing, the `never` marker, `build_stage`, the `node_synced`
deviation, and three README sentences each a little stronger than the code.

**Plugin and TUI UX (19).** The panel rendered a stale balance as current and ran a plain `doctor`
for a `--only` hint. In the TUI: three overlay heights each one row short — including the confirm
modal's typed-word echo, so the TUI's `--yes` was typed blind — two false keymap rows, a log search
destroyed once a second by its own follow poll, two blank panes at 70x20 where the only error
surface belongs, a clipped `fmonero setup --unit`, an unscannable receive QR (both halves: the
canvas's both-lit cells and the CLI's uncoloured glyphs), a truncated monetary column, an
off-screen account selector, two panes describing a state the same screen contradicts, a stale
exit code surviving a recovery, a banner offering `history | clear-pending`, a `setup --units`
failure that kept the settings it had already rewritten, two DESIGN.md section numbers in
user-facing copy, and a log title with no room for `follow on/off`.

One part of ux-4 was **not** taken: the TUI_POLISH_PLAYBOOK's `current/total` match count on the
Log screen's search row. It is a feature request rather than the finding's failure scenario (the
search being wiped), and §7a does not ask for it.

### Every RED row is still RED

Nothing in this phase installed Monero, started a daemon, or broadcast anything. `monerod` and
`monero-wallet-rpc` are still absent (§1.1), every §4b RED row is still RED, and the three honesty
surfaces say so — `build_stage` is now `phase-3-integrated` with the doctor and TUI added to its
GREEN and AMBER sentences and one clause **added** to its RED sentence (every doctor check in the
node, wallet and storage groups against a live daemon), and fourteen of the thirty-six
`capabilities.commands` rows read `verified: "red"`. The one real `scripts/install.sh --yes` is the
Phase 4 **verifier's**, not the fixer's, and it has not been run here.

## Phase 4 verification (real output)

DESIGN.md §8 Phase 4, the verifier's half: `scripts/install.sh --yes` twice — the one real `setup`
run §4b reserves for this phase — the bar widget confirmed loading, `scripts/check.sh --full`,
`tests/e2e.sh --fast` and `--full` re-run, §4b refilled from this run's output, and an honest
report of what still fails.

Run on 2026-09-17 in `/home/oliver/Projects/odcpw/fmonero` on `main`, at the tree this entry lands
with. **No Monero binary is installed and `sudo` needs a password** (§1.1): `command -v monerod`
and `command -v monero-wallet-rpc` both answer nothing, so every §4b RED row is still RED and
nothing below claims one. Nothing in this phase broadcast a transaction, started a `monerod`, or
opened a wallet file.

### The state of the machine before the run, because it was not pristine

An `install.sh --yes` had already been run at **05:48**, five minutes after the Phase 4 fixer's
last commit (`46afd9f`, 05:43) and after its entry was written — so that entry's closing sentence
("The one real `scripts/install.sh --yes` is the Phase 4 **verifier's** … and it has not been run
here") is true of the fixer and false of the machine. `DECISIONS.md` is append-only (§2j), so this
paragraph is the correction rather than an edit.

What was on disk at 06:07, before this phase's first run (sha256 truncated to 16, then mode):

```
e07cb3d30b4ef695  755  ~/.local/bin/fmonero
3c793e486bcaad34  600  ~/.config/systemd/user/fmonero-monerod.service
8faf1960a33f933e  600  ~/.config/systemd/user/fmonero-wallet-rpc.service
bed63c9603dc9f5c  600  ~/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf
d375efcb2ff80fc9  644  ~/.config/omarchy/extensions/omarchy-menu.jsonc
```

A first run onto that state would have reported every action `unchanged` and the idempotence
assertion §8 asks for would have been vacuous — "the second run reports what the first run
reported" proves nothing when the first run did nothing. So **the two unit files were moved into
the scratchpad and deleted from `~/.config/systemd/user/` before the first run**, and nothing else
was touched: the credential file was left alone (deleting it would draw a fresh `rpc-login` and
break the byte comparison), and the Omarchy menu file was left alone (it is user-owned and holds
entries that are not fmonero's). That is the whole of the setup, stated so that the transition the
first run shows is attributable.

**The assertion §8 asks for does not fully hold, and here is exactly how far it does.** Five of the

3c793e486bcaad34  600  ~/.config/systemd/user/fmonero-monerod.service   # byte-identical to the
8faf1960a33f933e  600  ~/.config/systemd/user/fmonero-wallet-rpc.service # 05:48 copies, cmp -s

### The bar widget loads, and polls this binary

`omarchy restart shell` at 06:09:23, then:

$ omarchy-shell oliver.monero cursor
{"section":"header","index":0,"active":false,"opened":false,

`alive: true` **is** the answer to "does its `Service` poll `fmonero status --json` without
errors": §6.3 derives `alive` from `status.ok === true`, so the widget spawned
`[bin, "status", "--json"]`, got exit 0 and parsed the schema-1 document. `binaryMissing: false`
is `which fmonero` succeeding on the shell's PATH — the Phase 3 capture of this same probe read
`alive:false, binaryMissing:true`, because `~/.local/bin/fmonero` did not exist until this run.
`iconCase: 1` is §6.4's second row (a warning of severity `error`), which is `monero_not_installed`
— the honest icon for this machine. `sections` now includes `warnings`, which the Phase 3 capture
did not: that section is only built when a warning exists and the poll succeeded.

§2l, re-measured with §1.4's `date +%s%N` loop on the installed binary:

**All ten failures are one defect class, and it is the harness rather than the product.** Every one
of them asserts a premise that the run §8 assigns to this phase destroys: *nothing is installed*.
The proof is a control run of the whole suite under a scratch `HOME`, with `CARGO_HOME` and
`RUSTUP_HOME` left pointing at the real ones:

```
$ env HOME=$SCRATCH/fakehome XDG_CONFIG_HOME=… XDG_STATE_HOME=… XDG_DATA_HOME=… \
      XDG_CACHE_HOME=… CARGO_HOME=~/.cargo RUSTUP_HOME=~/.rustup ~/.cargo/bin/cargo test

```

219 tests, none failing, on the same tree. The five:

| Test | Asserts | Why it fails now |
|---|---|---|
| `doctor::checks::the_one_line_predicates_answer_as_the_table_says` | `binary_on_path(&p).reason == Some("not_installed")` | `p` is built from the real machine, where `~/.local/bin/fmonero` now exists |
| `doctor::mutate::under_a_test_state_dir_no_fixer_may_reach_outside_it` | `!unit.exists()` after the chokepoint refuses | the refusal still works; the unit exists because `setup` wrote it |
| `tui::node_mutations_are_refused_when_fmonero_does_not_own_the_node` | `u`/`d`/`L` open no modal "without a user unit" | there **is** a user unit now, so §2i legitimately allows them |
| `status::with_both_daemons_absent_the_document_is_all_null_and_still_ok` | `node.layout == "none"` | it is `"user_unit"`, which is the correct answer |
| `units::a_dry_run_plans_the_three_files_and_redacts_the_credential` | all three files `planned` | the two units are `unchanged`, which is the point of idempotence |

and the five `tests/e2e.sh` assertions:

```
  FAIL  node up with no units is exit 4 (§4b): expected exit 4, got 8
  FAIL  unit_missing: expected reason unit_missing, got null
  FAIL  wallet open with a password but no unit is exit 4 (§4b): expected exit 4, got 8
  FAIL  it lists a CreateDir action for wallet.dir when it is absent (§4.2): … = false
  FAIL  §4.2: --wallet-dir mismatch     unit: (empty)  setting: …/wallets/mainnet
```

The last one is worth its own sentence: it reads the unit's `--wallet-dir` out of the **diff** of
`setup --units --dry-run`, and an `unchanged` action carries `"diff": null` by contract (§5b
`FileAction`), so the grep has nothing to read. §8's Phase 1b gate line chose the `--dry-run`
output precisely because "a dry run writes no file"; the case it did not anticipate is the file
being there and already correct. Each of the four affected rows was re-verified by hand below, so
the matrix is filled from output that exists rather than from an assertion that could not run.

**Nothing here was fixed.** §8 gives this phase the verification and the honesty surfaces, and the
brief it ran under says a false claim is fixed while a bug is reported. The five tests and the five
assertions are bugs; the fixes are named in the findings.

### Findings — reported, not fixed

**1. `node up` and `wallet open` never check that the Monero binary exists, so on a machine that
`setup` has configured and Monero has not reached, they wait 30 s and exit 8 instead of exiting 4
`not_installed` — and leave the unit retrying every 5 s.** This is the state `install.sh --yes`
leaves on this machine, so it is the first thing the owner will meet.

```
$ time fmonero node up --json
{"code":8,"code_name":"timeout","error":"fmonero-monerod.service did not answer get_info within 30s",
 "hint":"fmonero status --json","ok":false,"reason":null,"schema":1}
30 s

$ systemctl --user status fmonero-monerod.service
Active: activating (auto-restart) (Result: exit-code)
Process: ExecStart=/usr/bin/monerod --config-file … (code=exited, status=203/EXEC)
```

`203/EXEC` is systemd saying the binary is not there. §4a assigns that condition exit **4**
(`backend`: "`monerod`/`monero-wallet-rpc` not installed, unit missing, …") with the hint
`omarchy pkg add monero`; exit 8 is defined as "nothing is broken" and its hint
(`fmonero status --json`) points at a document that will say `reachable: false` without saying why.
§4.2 spends a paragraph on exactly this failure shape for `wallet.dir` — "`Restart=on-failure`
retries it every 5 s into systemd's start limit, and every fmonero command reports 'the wallet
daemon is not running' — the symptom pointing away from the cause" — and the same shape is here,
one layer up. Both restart loops were stopped by hand after every run in this phase
(`systemctl --user stop`), and both units are `inactive` and `disabled` as this entry lands.
**Fix:** one `proc::which` in `node_session::up` and in `units::ensure_running`, before
`units::start`, returning `Error::backend(…).with_reason("not_installed")` with the
`omarchy pkg add monero` hint — the same probe the doctor's `monerod_installed` already makes.
It also restores §4b's two GREEN rows, which currently read `unit_missing` for a case that is
really `not_installed`.

**2. `setup --plugin` runs `omarchy plugin enable` and `omarchy bar put` on every invocation and
reports them `written` whether or not anything changed.** §4.2 says "each skipped when already
done", and `setup.rs`'s own comment above the loop repeats it —
`// argv only, never a shell string (§2f), and each step is skipped when already done` — but the
loop has no pre-check: it spawns both commands and maps `out.success()` to `FileStatus::Written`.
The commands are themselves idempotent, so the machine is unharmed (one `oliver.monero` entry,
same position, verified above); what is wrong is the report, and the report is what §8's
idempotence assertion reads. **Fix:** ask `omarchy plugin list` whether the id is enabled and
`shell.json` whether it is in the bar layout, and emit `FileStatus::Unchanged` without spawning —
`menu_step` and `plugin_step`'s symlink branch already work that way.

**3. `Paths` has no seam for `systemd_user_dir`, so no test can make `units::detect_layout` see a
machine without units.** `Paths::discover` sets it from the real `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`
(`paths.rs:92`), `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` is read only by the doctor's `probes.rs` (a Phase 2
deviation, recorded there), and `tests/e2e.sh`'s `sandbox()` redirects state, config and cache but
not this. That is why the ten failures above exist at all, and why pointing
`FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` at an empty directory changed nothing:

```
$ env FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR=$SCRATCH/nounits tests/e2e.sh --fast
e2e.sh (fast): 326 passed, 5 failed, 2 skipped        # identical
```

**Fix:** move the override into `Paths::discover` under the same `under_test_state_dir()` guard the
`FMONERO_PROC_ROOT` seam already uses, have `probes.rs::systemd_user_dir` read the field instead of
the environment, and add one line to `sandbox()`. It is one field, and it is the difference between
a suite that survives its own installer and a suite that has to be run on a machine nobody has used.
A second, independent hole stays open even then, and finding 1 is what makes it visible: systemd's
user manager holds a unit it has already loaded, so `units::start` can succeed against a unit file
no longer on disk. `wallet open` with no unit file is exit 8, not the exit 4 §4b's GREEN row names,
even under a config home with no units at all — reproduced below.

**4. Still open from the Phase 4 fixer, unchanged: `secrets-5`,** the node unit's
`--config-file %h/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf` against the XDG-resolved `paths.monerod_conf()`.
This machine has `XDG_CONFIG_HOME=/home/oliver/.config`, so the two agree and the installed unit is
correct; the divergence needs a non-default `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and §4.2 prescribes the literal, so
it remains a contract change. The `ux-19` TUI line budget is likewise carried forward untouched
(4 400 lines against §7a's ≤ 1 800); this phase added nothing to those files.

### The four rows the harness could not reach, re-verified by hand

With `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` pointed at a directory that has no `systemd/user` (real `HOME`, so the
disk-floor check still sees `/home`'s 238 GB), and the state, config and cache dirs sandboxed:

```
$ fmonero node up --json
{"code":4,"code_name":"backend","error":"no fmonero node unit is installed",
 "hint":"fmonero setup --units","ok":false,"reason":"unit_missing","schema":1}
exit: 4                                     # §4b GREEN row: holds

$ FMONERO_WALLET_PASSWORD=x fmonero wallet open somewallet --json
{"code":8,"code_name":"timeout","error":"fmonero-wallet-rpc.service started but did not answer within 30s",
 "hint":"fmonero doctor --only units","ok":false,"reason":null,"schema":1}
exit: 8                                     # §4b GREEN row: DOES NOT HOLD — finding 3
```

`wallet open` is the one matrix row this phase could not make true. `units::ensure_running` probes,
fails, and calls `units::start`, which is `systemctl --user start fmonero-wallet-rpc.service` — and
systemd answers from the unit it loaded at daemon-reload, not from the directory fmonero is looking
at. So the row's exit 4 is reachable only on a machine whose systemd user manager has never seen
the unit. It is recorded below as GREEN-with-a-precondition rather than quietly kept.

The `setup` rows, against a sandboxed config where `node.network` is `stagenet`, which is also the
first exercise of §4's network substitution table:

```
$ fmonero settings set node.network stagenet --json
{"key":"node.network","new":"stagenet","ok":true,"old":"mainnet","schema":1}
$ fmonero setup --units --dry-run --json | jq -r '.actions[] | "\(.status)  \(.what)"'
planned  settings node.url: http://127.0.0.1:18081 -> http://127.0.0.1:38081
planned  settings wallet.url: http://127.0.0.1:18083 -> http://127.0.0.1:38083
planned  settings wallet.port: 18083 -> 38083
planned  settings wallet.dir: …/wallets/mainnet -> …/wallets/stagenet
planned  CreateDir wallet.dir
unchanged  node unit
planned  wallet unit
planned  monerod.conf
$ # the rendered hunks
+  --rpc-bind-port 38083 \
+  --wallet-dir /home/oliver/.local/share/fmonero/wallets/stagenet \
+  --daemon-address 127.0.0.1:38081 \
+  --stagenet \
+rpc-bind-port=38081
+stagenet=1
```

`CreateDir wallet.dir` is present, so that §4.2 row holds; the node unit is `unchanged` because it
is network-independent (its `ExecStart` names only `--config-file`), which is correct. And
`--rotate-rpc-login --dry-run` against the real conf, which writes nothing:

```
$ fmonero setup --units --rotate-rpc-login --dry-run --json | jq -r '.actions[] | select(.what=="monerod.conf") | .status, (.diff // "no diff")'
planned
--- current
+++ rendered
@@ -20,0 +20,0 @@
$ sha256sum ~/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf | cut -c1-16
bed63c9603dc9f5c                            # unchanged, as a dry run must leave it
```

The status is `planned`, not `unchanged`, so the rotation is real; the diff body is empty because
the only differing line is `rpc-login=`, which §4.2 requires the displayed diff to redact — after
redaction the two sides are equal. Correct, and worth knowing before someone reads the empty hunk
as "nothing would change".

### The doctor, on the machine `install.sh` left

The §4b GREEN row, and for the first time with real artefacts under the `units`, `install` and
`security` groups rather than a sandbox:

```
$ fmonero doctor --json | jq .summary
{"total":37,"pass":17,"warn":0,"fail":2,"skip":18,"fixable":0,"fixed":0,"fix_failed":0,
 "planned":0,"generated_at":"2026-09-17T06:20:34+02:00","fix":false,"dry_run":false,"only":null,
 "run_dir":null,"failing":["monerod_installed","wallet_rpc_installed"],"exit_code":11,
 "next":["fmonero doctor --json | jq '.checks[] | select(.status != \"pass\")'"]}
exit: 11
```

The two failures are `fail:not_installed`, every `node`/`wallet`/`storage` check is a
`skip:<reason>` naming `monerod_not_installed` or `wallet_rpc_not_installed`, and the 17 passes are:

```
units/units_present · units/unit_loopback · units/unit_no_password_in_argv ·
units/unit_no_node_wants · units/unit_enabled_state · units/wallet_dir_matches_unit ·
units/wallet_port_consistent · install/binary_on_path · install/state_valid ·
install/settings_valid · install/history_cache_fresh · install/plugin_linked ·
install/plugin_enabled · install/menu_entries · security/monerod_conf_mode ·
security/state_perms · security/ports_owned
```

Before this run `binary_on_path`, `plugin_linked`, `plugin_enabled`, `menu_entries` and all seven
`units` checks had never been answered against anything but a fixture. `status` on the same
machine, for the record:

```
$ fmonero status --json | jq -c '{ok, node:{layout,reachable,height}, wallet:{unit_active,reachable,open}, pending, warnings:[.warnings[]|{id,severity}]}'
{"ok":true,"node":{"layout":"user_unit","reachable":false,"height":null},
 "wallet":{"unit_active":false,"reachable":false,"open":false},"pending":null,
 "warnings":[{"id":"monero_not_installed","severity":"error"},{"id":"node_down","severity":"warn"},
             {"id":"wallet_down","severity":"warn"}]}
exit: 0
```

`layout: "user_unit"` is new — every earlier phase saw `"none"`, because no unit existed.

class any of its scenarios reaches (§4b). "README?" is what README's Status section says about the
row: `GREEN`/`AMBER` mean it is listed under that heading, `RED` means it is named as unverified.

**133 rows: 44 GREEN · 66 AMBER · 20 RED · 1 "GREEN (precondition)" · 1 "partly GREEN" · 1 "bug".**
The last three are spelled out rather than rounded into a class: `wallet open` with no unit is GREEN
only on a machine whose systemd user manager has never loaded the unit, the second `setup` run is
`unchanged` for five of seven actions, and `node up` against an installed unit with no `monerod` is
a defect rather than a verification result. Every command of §4.1 appears at least once.

### Every RED row is still RED, and the exact commands that turn them GREEN

Nothing in this phase installed Monero, started a daemon, opened a wallet file or broadcast
anything. `omarchy pkg add monero` needs the owner's password, which no agent on this machine has
(§1.1). §4b's rehearsal list, unchanged, on **stagenet** first — stagenet coins are free from a
faucet, so the send path is exercised the first time with nothing at stake:

```bash
omarchy pkg add monero                        # installs monerod + monero-wallet-rpc
scripts/install.sh --yes                      # already done by this phase; re-run is a no-op
fmonero settings set node.network stagenet    # ports 38080/38081/38083, wallets/stagenet
fmonero setup --units --yes                   # rewrites both units + monerod.conf (0600), stagenet
fmonero node up --wait --wait-timeout 86400   # expect: exit 0 once synchronized, or exit 8
fmonero wallet create stagetest --yes         # prompts twice, refuses an empty password
monero-wallet-cli --wallet-file ~/.local/share/fmonero/wallets/stagenet/stagetest --offline
                                              # then type: seed — WRITE IT DOWN BEFORE FUNDING
fmonero wallet open stagetest                 # prompts for the password, no echo
grep -c "$YOUR_TEST_PASSWORD" \
  ~/.local/state/fmonero/run/monero-wallet-rpc.log          # expect: 0  (§4.2 --log-level 0)
fmonero wallet address --qr                   # fund this from a stagenet faucet
fmonero wallet history --limit 5 --json
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --dry-run     # expect: a real fee, nothing relayed
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --yes         # THE row that matters; then check the txid in history
fmonero wallet send <addr> 0.01 --yes         # run it TWICE: proves the §2a.4 record self-clears
fmonero node restart --yes                    # RED row: a real restart against monerod
fmonero node down --yes                       # RED row: stop_daemon then systemctl --user stop
fmonero node up --wait --wait-timeout 3600    # RED row: a real start
S=$(date +%s%N); for i in $(seq 20); do fmonero status --json >/dev/null; done; E=$(date +%s%N)
echo "warm status mean: $(( (E-S)/20000000 ))ms"   # the §2l (c) figure; paste into DECISIONS.md
fmonero doctor --json | jq '.summary'
tests/e2e.sh --full
# then, only once every row above passed, repeat on mainnet:
fmonero settings set node.network mainnet && fmonero setup --units --yes
```

Two lines of that list are worth flagging before the owner runs it. `scripts/install.sh --yes` has
now been run, so it is a no-op apart from a rebuild; and `fmonero node up` will keep answering exit
8 after 30 s until finding 1 is fixed or `monerod` is on PATH — with Monero installed it answers
honestly, so the finding does not block the rehearsal.

### The honesty surfaces, updated to match this matrix

Three files, and nothing else:

- `crates/fmonero-core/src/agent.rs` — `BUILD_STAGE` is `phase-4-verified`; `BUILD_NOTE`'s GREEN
  sentence gains the real `setup --all --yes` run, the bar widget loading in the live shell and the
  doctor's three real-artefact groups; its RED sentence **drops "a real setup run"** and gains
  `setup`'s two unexercised refusals, the 120 GB disk floor, and one sentence naming finding 1 as
  known and unfixed. `RED_COMMANDS` keeps `setup` — §4b's rule is the lowest class any scenario
  reaches, and the `system_unit` and disk-floor refusals are still unexercised — with the comment
  rewritten so the reason is the new one rather than the old one. The clause list in
  `verified_is_the_lowest_class_every_scenario_reaches` follows.
- `README.md` — the Install section's "**`--yes` has never been run on this machine**" is now false
  and is replaced by what the two runs actually reported, including finding 2; "those three commands
  are unverified" is narrowed to the two that still are, and names finding 1; the Status intro no
  longer says the section was written at the API-freeze gate; the GREEN list gains the real setup
  run, the widget, the units' `--wallet-dir` equality, the stagenet render and the doctor's 17
  passes, and loses the flat claim that `wallet open` with no unit is exit 4; the RED list drops
  "A real `setup` run" and names `setup`'s two refusals instead.
- `docs/ROBOT.md` is unchanged and still byte-identical to `fmonero robot-docs`, because
  `ROBOT_GUIDE` did not need to change.

`scripts/check.sh`'s §2j scan passes on the edited text — 1 466 lines of README, `--help`,
`robot-docs`, `version --json` and `capabilities --json`, no unmarked claim — with all four of its
self-tests firing, including the one that proves an adjacent "never" does not excuse a RED claim.
The `(every|each) step is idempotent` and `(wires up|sets up)` alternatives the Phase 4 fixer added
to that pattern are **kept** even though a real `setup` run is no longer RED: finding 2 means a
blanket idempotence claim would still be wrong, and relaxing a check is not this phase's to do.

### What this phase did not do

It did not fix the five non-hermetic tests, the five `tests/e2e.sh` assertions, finding 1 or
finding 2 — §8 gives the verifier the verification and the honesty surfaces, and the brief it ran
under says a false claim is fixed while a bug is reported. It did not install Monero, start a
daemon, open or create a wallet, broadcast anything, run `sudo`, or push. It did stop, by hand,
both systemd user units that `tests/e2e.sh`'s `node up` and `wallet open` cases started during the
runs above; both are `inactive` and `disabled` as this entry lands, and the machine's fmonero
artefacts are byte-identical to what `install.sh --yes` wrote.

## Fix-up after the Phase 4 verification — design brief v5.1 (2026-09-17)

The Phase 4 verifier reported ten failing assertions, three findings and two items the Phase 4
fixer had deferred because closing them needed a change to DESIGN.md. Every one of them is closed
here. The orchestrator authorised three amendments to the contract; they are recorded first,
because the rest of this entry depends on them.

### The authorised DESIGN.md amendments

- 2026-09-17 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v5.1: **§4.2 renders the node unit's `--config-file` as
  the absolute, XDG-resolved `paths.monerod_conf()`**, exactly as the wallet unit already renders
  `--wallet-dir`, closing `secrets-5`. systemd expands `%h` to `$HOME` and has **no specifier for
  `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`**, so with that variable set anywhere but `~/.config` the `%h` literal named a
  file `setup --units` never wrote: `monerod` treats a missing *named* config file as fatal
  (§1b.6), `Restart=on-failure` retried it, and `node up` answered exit 8 "did not answer
  `get_info` within 30 s" — the symptom pointing away from the cause. v5 kept the literal on the
  grounds that it was "likewise uncompared", and that was the defect. The `%h`/`%S` guard's wording
  now says what it has always been about (the **conf** file, where boost expands nothing); `%S`
  stays in the wallet unit's `StateDirectory=`/`WorkingDirectory=`/`--log-file`, and the node unit
  carries no specifier at all. The doctor's `units_present` — a byte-for-byte comparison against
  `units::render_*` — is named in §7.1 as the upgrade path for a unit written by an older build,
  and the five §7.1 node-unit fixtures carry the absolute form with the reason in their provenance
  header.
- 2026-09-17 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v5.1: **§7a's line budget becomes the measured figure,
  `≤ 4 600 lines across src/tui/{mod,theme,screens,send,subs}.rs` (`tests.rs` excluded)**, closing
  `ux-19` without deleting a feature. fproton's own TUI missed its 1 200-line budget by the same
  2.2× ratio, and every screen, key and modal §7a specifies is implemented — so the honest lever
  was the number, not the feature set: closing `ux-19` by deleting a specified screen would have
  traded a missed budget for a missing feature. 4 600 is the measured 4 557 rounded up; the budget
  is a ceiling against growth now rather than a target, and `scripts/check.sh` fails above it. The
  Phase 2 TUI gate, the Phase 3 ledger and the Phase 4 fixer each reported the overrun and none
  enforced it; an unenforced budget is not a budget.
- 2026-09-17 · orchestrator · DESIGN.md v5.1: **§2i gains `units::preflight`** — before any
  `systemctl` call, `node up`, `node restart`, `wallet open` and `wallet create` check that the
  unit file exists in `paths.systemd_user_dir` (else exit 4 `unit_missing`, hint
  `fmonero setup --units --yes`) and that the daemon binary is on `PATH` (else exit 4
  `not_installed`, hint `omarchy pkg add monero`), both within milliseconds. §4a assigns that
  condition exit 4; the old answer was exit 8 after thirty seconds with a unit left restarting
  every five, which was finding 1 and the first state an owner of this build would have met. A
  unit fmonero started that never answered is now stopped again (`systemctl --user stop`, then
  `reset-failed`) and the error says so, so no command can leave a `Restart=on-failure` loop
  behind it. §2i's seam list gains `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR`, §4.2's plugin bullet names its
  `shell.json` pre-check, §4b's `node up | unit installed, monerod absent` row becomes GREEN with
  exit 4 `not_installed`, and §4's exit-4 reason list already carried both names.

### The StartLimit decision, and its reason

Amendment 3 asked whether to add `StartLimitIntervalSec`/`StartLimitBurst` to the rendered units.
**Both units now set `StartLimitIntervalSec=60` and `StartLimitBurst=3`**, and the reason is that
this document twice describes a failing unit as retrying "into systemd's start limit" while the
manager's defaults make that impossible: `DefaultStartLimitIntervalSec=10s` with
`DefaultStartLimitBurst=5`, against `RestartSec=5`, is two starts per interval — the burst of five
is never reached and a `203/EXEC` restarts forever. That is exactly what the verifier met and had
to stop by hand, twice. Three tries in a minute, then `failed`, is the behaviour the prose already
claimed. The cost is that a unit which *has* hit the limit refuses the next `start` until its
failed state is cleared, so `stop_after_failed_start` runs `systemctl --user reset-failed` as well
— an owner who has just installed Monero must not meet a refusal earned before they did.

### Deviations this phase recorded (§2k)

1. **`units::preflight`'s binary check is skipped under `Systemctl::Fixture`.** The §2i seam
   already stands in for the machine's systemd, and a fixture that reports a unit as present and
   active is standing in for its daemon too — `fmonero-mock` is what answers the RPC in every one
   of those §4b AMBER rows, on a machine where no Monero binary exists. Without the exemption
   `node up under the user_unit layout` (e2e) and `node_up_wait_times_out_with_exit_eight`
   (`rpc::mock_tests`) would both answer exit 4 rather than what they are about, and the
   alternative — a fake `monerod` on `PATH` — cannot be done from an in-process test without
   mutating the environment of every other thread in the binary. The seam is honoured only under
   `FMONERO_STATE_DIR`, so no real invocation can reach the exemption;
   `units::tests::preflight_wants_a_unit_file_and_a_binary` drives the check directly with an
   explicit binary name, and `tests/e2e.sh` proves the real answer with no fixture set at all.
2. **`Settings::load` derives the default `wallet.dir` from `paths.wallet_dir`** rather than
   computing `~/.local/share/fmonero/wallets/<network>` from `$HOME` a second time. The two agreed
   only while `XDG_DATA_HOME` was `~/.local/share`, and `setup --units` derives its own `want_dir`
   from `paths.wallet_dir` (§4.2) — so anywhere else a machine that needed no change had
   `setup --units` planning one, and the §4.2 byte-equality assertion compared a rendered unit
   against a setting the dry run had not saved. One default, one authority.
3. **`Context::for_test` sandboxes `systemd_user_dir`, `local_bin` and `settings.wallet.dir`.**
   §5b does not say what `for_test` puts where; it said "every fmonero directory under `dir`", and
   these three are not fmonero's own. They are what five unit tests were unknowingly asserting
   about the machine, so they are under `dir` now.
4. **`BUILD_STAGE` is `phase-4-verified-and-fixed`.** The verifier's `phase-4-verified` was true
   of a tree whose own suite failed ten assertions on it.

### What was fixed, item by item

**1. `secrets-5` — the node unit's `--config-file`.** `units::render_node_unit` renders
`paths.monerod_conf()`, the installed unit carries
`--config-file /home/oliver/.config/fmonero/monerod.conf`, and
`the_units_carry_the_specifiers_and_the_literal_wallet_dir` now asserts that the node unit contains
no `%` at all. The five §7.1 node-unit fixtures carry the absolute form.

**2. `ux-19` — the TUI line budget.** Measured 4 557 lines across
`src/tui/{mod,theme,screens,send,subs}.rs`; `scripts/check.sh` fails above 4 600 and prints the
real number. The plugin's §6.1 budget got the same treatment while the gate was being written
(1 243 against 1 310) — §6.1 asks for the counts to be recorded and nothing was recording them.

**3. Finding 1 — `node up` and `wallet open` on a set-up machine with no Monero.**
`units::preflight`, called by `node_session::{up, restart}` after the layout gate and by
`units::ensure_running` after its probe. Exit 4 `not_installed` in 5 ms and 9 ms on this machine
(pasted below), no `systemctl` call, `NRestarts=0` afterwards.

**4. Finding 3 — the harness could not see a machine without units.**
`FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` moved into `Paths::discover` under the `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` guard;
`probes.rs::systemd_user_dir` reads the field; `tests/e2e.sh`'s `sandbox` exports it along with
`XDG_DATA_HOME` (for `wallet.dir`) and pins `node.data_dir` to a filesystem with room (for §1.3's
floor); `Context::for_test` puts `systemd_user_dir`, `local_bin` and `settings.wallet.dir` under
its own root.

**5. The ten failing assertions.** Each one failed on the pre-fix tree and passes now, and the
proof is a run of the suite at the verifier's own commit rather than a claim:

```
$ git stash push -m fixup-wip && cargo test --no-fail-fast          # at c245c92, the tree as the verifier left it
PRE-FIX: test doctor::mutate::tests::under_a_test_state_dir_no_fixer_may_reach_outside_it ... FAILED
PRE-FIX: test doctor::checks::tests::the_one_line_predicates_answer_as_the_table_says ... FAILED
PRE-FIX: test tui::tests::node_mutations_are_refused_when_fmonero_does_not_own_the_node ... FAILED
PRE-FIX: test result: FAILED. 72 passed; 3 failed
PRE-FIX: test units::tests::a_dry_run_plans_the_three_files_and_redacts_the_credential ... FAILED
PRE-FIX: test status::tests::with_both_daemons_absent_the_document_is_all_null_and_still_ok ... FAILED
PRE-FIX: test result: FAILED. 139 passed; 2 failed
$ git stash pop && cargo test
test result: ok. 76 passed; 0 failed      # crates/fmonero
test result: ok. 144 passed; 0 failed     # crates/fmonero-core
test result: ok. 3 passed; 0 failed       # crates/fmonero-mock
```

The five `tests/e2e.sh` assertions the verifier listed —
`node up with no units is exit 4`, `unit_missing`, `wallet open with a password but no unit is
exit 4`, `it lists a CreateDir action for wallet.dir when it is absent` and the `--wallet-dir`
grep — all pass in the run pasted below. Their **pre-fix** state is the verifier's own transcript
rather than a re-run here, and deliberately so: the pre-fix `wallet open` case reaches
`units::start` and starts the owner's real `fmonero-wallet-rpc.service`, which is what left it
flapping in the first place. Re-creating that to re-photograph a failure already recorded two
sections above would be the opposite of what this phase is for. The fifth was rewritten rather
than merely unblocked:
an `unchanged` action carries `"diff": null` by contract (§5b `FileAction`), so the grep had
nothing to read on a correct machine. It now reads the rendered unit out of a `planned` diff the
sandbox guarantees, **and** the installed unit file on disk, with the sandbox dropped — the row
the verifier had to check by hand.

**No assertion was weakened.** Every one of the five unit tests asserts exactly what it asserted
before; what changed is the directory it looks at. The three new ones —
`preflight_wants_a_unit_file_and_a_binary`, `each_unit_names_its_own_daemon`,
`a_unit_that_never_answered_is_stopped_again` — and the two new e2e steps are additions.

**6. Finding 2 — the two `omarchy` steps.** `plugin_on_bar` reads `~/.config/omarchy/shell.json`.
Second run: seven of seven `unchanged`, pasted below.

**7. The honesty surfaces.** `BUILD_STAGE` is `phase-4-verified-and-fixed`; `BUILD_NOTE`'s closing
sentence about finding 1 being known and unfixed is gone and its GREEN sentence names what
replaced it; `ROBOT_GUIDE` gains `4 not_installed` and `4 unit_missing`; `docs/ROBOT.md` is
regenerated and `tests/e2e.sh` asserts it byte-identical; README's Install and Status sections
report the run that happened. `scripts/check.sh`'s §2j scan passes on all of it, with its four
self-tests firing.

Both units are inactive and disabled as this entry lands, `NRestarts=0`, and nothing in this phase
had to stop one by hand — the two `systemctl --user stop` calls the verifier needed are exactly
what `units::stop_after_failed_start` now does for itself.

### The doctor and `status`, on the machine this left

```
$ fmonero doctor --json | jq -c .summary
{"total":37,"pass":17,"warn":0,"fail":2,"skip":18,"fixable":0,"fixed":0,"fix_failed":0,
 "planned":0,"generated_at":"2026-09-17T07:32:47+02:00","fix":false,"dry_run":false,"only":null,
 "run_dir":null,"failing":["monerod_installed","wallet_rpc_installed"],"exit_code":11,…}
exit: 11

$ fmonero doctor --json | jq -r '[.checks[]|select(.status=="pass")|(.group+"/"+.id)]|join(" · ")'
units/units_present · units/unit_loopback · units/unit_no_password_in_argv ·
units/unit_no_node_wants · units/unit_enabled_state · units/wallet_dir_matches_unit ·
units/wallet_port_consistent · install/binary_on_path · install/state_valid ·
install/settings_valid · install/history_cache_fresh · install/plugin_linked ·
install/plugin_enabled · install/menu_entries · security/monerod_conf_mode ·
security/state_perms · security/ports_owned

$ fmonero status --json | jq -c '{ok, node:{layout:.node.layout, reachable:.node.reachable, height:.node.height}, …}'
{"ok":true,"node":{"layout":"user_unit","reachable":false,"height":null},
 "wallet":{"unit_active":false,"reachable":false,"open":false},
 "units":{"node":true,"wallet":true},"pending":null,
 "warnings":[{"id":"monero_not_installed","severity":"error"},{"id":"node_down","severity":"warn"},
             {"id":"wallet_down","severity":"warn"}]}
exit: 0
```

`units/units_present` passing is worth one sentence: it is a byte-for-byte comparison against
`units::render_*`, so it is also the proof that the installed units carry v5.1's `--config-file`
and `StartLimit*` — and it is the upgrade path §7.1 now names for anyone whose units predate this
build.

§2l, re-measured with §1.4's `date +%s%N` loop on the installed binary:

```
(a) status, both daemons absent:  49 ms · 51 ms · 42 ms over three runs   (budget 60 ms, GREEN)
```

The Phase 4 verifier measured 29 ms. This is not a regression and it is worth saying why rather
than reporting the better number: the **pre-fix** binary, built from `c245c92` and measured on
this machine minutes later, answers in 50 ms too. The machine is busier than it was at 06:20.
**(b), (c) and (d) were not re-measured here** — (c) is RED and is never measured, and (b)/(d)
were not touched by any change in this phase.

### §4b verification matrix, refilled after the v5.1 fix-up

The Phase 4 verifier's table, re-filled rather than patched, because three of its rows were no
longer true and one of them was a defect report wearing a class name. **Every GREEN and AMBER row
below was exercised in this phase**, by `tests/e2e.sh --fast`/`--full` (378 passing assertions),
`cargo test` (223, and again under a scratch `HOME`), `tests/plugin-smoke.sh` (32),
`tests/tui_tmux.sh` (10), or by a command reproduced in this entry. The unit of the matrix is a
(command, scenario) pair, and a command's class in README is the lowest class any of its scenarios
reaches (§4b). "README?" is what README's Status section says about the row.

What changed from the verifier's table, and nothing else did:

- `node up | unit installed, monerod absent` was **bug**; it is GREEN, exit 4 `not_installed`.
- `node up | no node unit` and `wallet open | password, no unit` lose the
  "and none loaded by systemd" precondition the verifier had to write into them.
- `setup --all --yes | the second run's report` was **partly GREEN**; it is GREEN, 7 of 7.
- `setup --units | the §1.3 free-space floor refusal` was **RED**; it is GREEN. It is a local
  `statvfs` measurement and never needed a daemon — only a test willing to point `node.data_dir`
  at a filesystem under the floor.
- Four rows are new: `node restart` and `wallet open` against an absent binary, a failed start
  leaving nothing flapping, the suite having no side effect, and the suite passing under a
  never-set-up `HOME`.
- `the §1.3 disk floor` is renamed `as a NUMBER`, because the refusal it drives is no longer RED
  and the figure still is.

| a failed start | a unit fmonero started that never answered | GREEN | `units::tests::a_unit_that_never_answered_is_stopped_again` | `systemctl --user stop` + `reset-failed`, the error says so, and the unit is inactive again — the flapping the verifier had to undo by hand | GREEN |
| `tests/e2e.sh` | the whole run, on a machine that has been set up | GREEN | its own last step, on both runs | both of the owner's units `inactive`, `NRestarts=0` — no case reaches `systemctl --user start` on a real unit | GREEN |
| `cargo test` · `check.sh --full` · `e2e.sh --fast` | under a scratch `HOME` that has never been set up | GREEN | pasted above | 223 tests, 52 checks, 351 assertions, 0 failures — and the scratch `HOME` is **empty** afterwards | GREEN |

**138 rows: 53 GREEN · 66 AMBER · 19 RED.** No row is a "bug", a "precondition" or a "partly";
the three the verifier had to spell out that way are closed. Every command of §4.1 still appears
at least once.

### Every RED row is still RED

Nothing in this phase installed Monero, started a daemon, opened a wallet file or broadcast
anything. `omarchy pkg add monero` needs the owner's password, which no agent on this machine has
(§1.1). §4b's rehearsal list is unchanged and still runs on **stagenet** first; two of the
verifier's footnotes to it no longer apply:

- `scripts/install.sh --yes` has now been run four more times and is a no-op apart from a rebuild.
- `fmonero node up` no longer "keeps answering exit 8 after 30 s until finding 1 is fixed". It
  answers exit 4 `not_installed` in 5 ms, and the moment `omarchy pkg add monero` has run it
  proceeds to the §2i single-writer check as §4.1 describes.

The RED list itself shrank by exactly one entry — `setup`'s free-space refusal — and `setup` stays
a RED **command** for the one scenario nothing can exercise here: its refusal under Arch's
`system_unit` layout, which needs Arch's own `monerod.service` on the machine.

### What this phase did not do

It did not install Monero, start a daemon, create or open a wallet, broadcast anything, run
`sudo`, or push. It did not touch a `Cargo.toml` (§2k) or change any §5b signature: `preflight`,
`unit_binary`, `stop_after_failed_start`, `RESTART_SEC` and `Paths::omarchy_shell_json` are
additions with consumers (§5b rule 1), and `probes.rs::systemd_user_dir` kept its signature while
losing its environment read. It did not re-measure §2l (b), (c) or (d): (c) is RED and is never
measured, and nothing in this phase is on the path either of the other two times. It did not
relitigate `ux-19` by deleting a screen, and it did not relax a single assertion to make a test
pass — the five that failed assert exactly what they asserted before, against directories that
belong to the test rather than to the machine.
