  ### DEFECT A — the tab strip's hit rects are two columns per tab too far left (Wide/Medium)

  The owner's complaint (2) named the tab strip first, and §7a.2 calls itself "the fix for *the tabs
  look clickable and are not*". On a real 150×40 pty, with real SGR-1006 mouse reports written to
  the pane, it is still not. One click per column, each from the same starting frame, 0.9 s settle:

  ```
  strip:  [ 1 Node ]  2 Wallet    3 Send    4 Log
          123456789012345678901234567890123456789

  col  5  renders 'N' (Node)    -> Node      ok
  col 10  renders ']'           -> Wallet    WRONG
  col 13  renders '2'           -> Wallet    ok
  col 16  renders 'a' (Wallet)  -> Wallet    ok
  col 20  renders 't' (Wallet)  -> Send      WRONG
  col 25  renders '3'           -> Send      ok
  col 27  renders 'S' (Send)    -> Log       WRONG
  col 28  renders 'e' (Send)    -> Log       WRONG
  col 30  renders 'd' (Send)    -> Log       WRONG
  col 35  renders '4' (Log)     -> nothing   WRONG
  col 37  renders 'L' (Log)     -> nothing   WRONG
  col 39  renders 'g' (Log)     -> nothing   WRONG
  ```

  **The mechanism, from both sides of the boundary.** `ftui_widgets::Tabs::tab_label`
  (`ftui-widgets-0.7.0/src/tabs.rs:239-256`) wraps **every** tab in one leading and one trailing
  cell — `[`/`]` when it is active, a space on each side when it is not — so a tab occupies
  `title.width() + 2` columns. `tab_strip`'s hit loop
  (`crates/fmonero/src/tui/screens.rs:198-208`) advances `x` by `label.width()`:

  ```rust
  let w = label.width() as u16;          // 8 for " 1 Node ", 10 for " 2 Wallet "
  m.hits.tab(i, Rect::new(x, cols[0].y, w, 1));
  x += w;                                // the 2 decoration columns are never counted
  ```

  so every rect is 2 columns narrow and starts `2 × index` columns left of its label:

  | tab | drawn at | registered at |
  |---|---|---|
  | Node | 1–10 | 1–8 |
  | Wallet | 11–22 | 9–18 |
  | Send | 23–32 | 19–26 |
  | Log | 33–41 | 27–33 |

  which is the measured table above, cell for cell. The comment above that loop — *"the labels'
  widths are the literals above, so the rects are computed from the same source as the text"* — is
  the bug stated as a reassurance: the literals are the source of the **title**, not of the drawn
  width. The fix is `label.width() as u16 + 2`, and the honest version of it also stops asserting
  that the two agree, because `Tabs` owns that number and this module cannot see it.

  **The Narrow tier is correct**, which locates the fault precisely: there `tab_strip` draws its own
  `1·2·3·4` spans and registers its own 1×1 rects, and on a 56×24 pty clicks at columns 1, 3, 5, 7
  select Node, Wallet, Send, Log in order. Only the tier that delegates drawing to `Tabs` is wrong.

  **Why the suite is green.** `tui::tests::a_click_on_each_tab_switches_to_it` passes, and so does
  every other mouse case, because `find()` (`crates/fmonero/src/tui/tests.rs:928-936`) obtains the
  coordinate by scanning **`m.hits.at(x, y, None)`** — the same map the test then clicks. A map that
  disagrees with the rendered frame is perfectly self-consistent, so the suite proves routing and
  can never prove alignment. §7a.2 already worried about the wrong half of this: it spends four
  paragraphs establishing that `register_hit` does nothing under `App::fullscreen` and that the
  layout cache must therefore be hand-built — and then tests the hand-built cache against itself.
  The assertion that was missing is the one this session made by hand: **find the label in the
  rendered frame, click the label, and check where you land.** That is the same lesson as the
  previous round's finding 2 (assert the rendered sentence, not the side effect), in the one place
  where §7a.2's whole premise — that the two input methods are peers — is decided.

  ### DEFECT B — the picker elides the address it is required never to elide

  §7a.4: **address** "never elided, for the reason the send confirm never elides an address", and
  the implementer's own gate says "*columns now drop from the right until the address has 26, and
  'the address is never elided at any tier' is a behaviour rather than a sentence*". At the exact
  geometry this brief asked for, it is a sentence. Address cell as rendered, one TUI resized across
  widths, the configured node being the owner's own:

  ```
  width  90   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet]            ELIDED
  width 100   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081 ] ok
  width 120   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081]  ok
  width 150   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.co]         ELIDED   <- the brief's own 150x40
  width 180   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:1808]   ELIDED
  width 200   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081 ] ok
  width 240   [ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081 ] ok
  ```

  Two independent causes, in `screens.rs:589-591`:

  ```rust
  const ADDRESS_MIN: u16 = 26;
  const COL: u16 = 12;
  while header.len() > 1 && rows[0].width < ADDRESS_MIN + COL * (header.len() as u16 - 1) { header.pop(); }
  ```

  · the budget counts the columns but not the `header.len() - 1` single-cell separators `Table`
    draws between them, so a loop that stops "with 26 left for the address" actually leaves
    `26 - (n-1)`; at six columns that is 21, and the address pane is ~91 wide at 150×40, which is
    how a 150-column terminal produces a worse address column than a 100-column one. · and 26 is
    itself too small: `● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081` is **31** columns, so even a loop whose
    arithmetic were right would elide the address this machine is configured with. The measured
    figure for the address the owner actually has is the one to budget against, exactly as §7a's own
    budget rule says about line counts.

  ### DEFECT C — the help overlay's first row is false for half of its own key list

  `KEYS[0]` (`crates/fmonero/src/tui/mod.rs:131-137`) is rendered verbatim in the `?` overlay:

  ```
  Tab  ⇧Tab  1 2 3 4    screens — always, even inside a field or a modal
  ```

  and carries the comment *"it is the one row whose text has to be true everywhere"*. For `Tab` and
  `⇧Tab` it is true, everywhere, and this session proved it in 26 cells of a real-pty matrix. For
  `1 2 3 4` it is false the moment a field has focus, on the money screen, with real digits:

  ```
  # Send screen, Amount field focused, "0.01" typed, then the keys 2 and 4:
  amount before        : │ Amount  0.01
  after pressing 2, 4  : │ Amount  0.0124        (and the screen did not change)
  after pressing q     : │ Amount  0.0124q       (and the TUI did not quit)
  ```

  `send.rs:94`'s own comment states the behaviour — *"`Esc` leaves it so that `1 2 3 4`, `q` and `?`
  work"* — and the Send screen's status line tells the truth when unfocused (`form unfocused —
  1..4 switch screens, Enter returns`). So the code is coherent and the **overlay** is the thing
  that lies, in the one row §7a.1 wrote to be believed. This is not a reopening of complaint (1):
  `Tab` is resolved above every branch and always works, which is what makes the trap gone. It is
  §2j about the sentence next to it. Either the row says `Tab ⇧Tab` always and `1 2 3 4` when no
  field has focus, or the Send screen stops focusing a field on entry — the first is a text change
  and the second is a behaviour change, and the row and the behaviour currently disagree whichever
  is chosen.

  ### FINDING D — the status badge says `down` for a daemon it is itself starting

  `wallet_badge` (`mod.rs:582-597`) reads `idle` only when the `wallet_idle` warning is up, which
  §5 raises when the **unit is inactive**. The moment the unit goes active and the RPC has not yet
  answered a poll, the badge falls through to `down` — including while the TUI's own action is the
  reason. Both states caught live on one screen:

  ```
  status 1.2s after W : ● node remote ● wallet down │ ⠙ starting the wallet daemon 1s / 30s · Esc cancels
  the panel two lines up:                             wallet daemon: starting…

  during a TUI create : ● node remote ● wallet down │ ⠙ creating scratchver 25s / 300s · Esc cancels
  the panel two lines up:                             wallet daemon: up
  ```

  Same family as the previous round's finding 1 (`wallet down` where §5 said `wallet_idle`), one
  state further along: the badge has no term for "up and busy", and `syncing` — which it does use
  for reachable-and-nothing-open — would be closer than `down` for a poll that timed out while the
  daemon was working.

  ### FINDING E — the `Node` → `Nodes` menu label can never reach a machine that already installed

  `crates/fmonero/assets/menu.jsonc` renames `monero.node`'s label to `Nodes`, and §4.2's merge
  "never rewrites what is already there" — deliberately, to protect a user's own edits. So on the
  owner's machine `scripts/install.sh --yes` added exactly one entry and reported so itself
  (`1 Omarchy menu entries`): `monero.wallet` → `Wallet` → `fmonero tui --wallet`. `monero.node` is
  still labelled **`Node`**, and no `setup` invocation will ever change it. "The menu entries gain
  Wallet and Nodes" is therefore half deliverable; the other half needs either a one-line hand edit
  or a merge that may update a label it previously wrote.

  ### What was proved to work, on a real pty, with real input

  **Navigation (complaint 1) — fixed.** A hand-written matrix on `tmux new-session -d -x 150 -y 40`,
  driving a real pty and reading `capture-pane` back, with a wallet open against `fmonero-mock` so
  the Wallet screen's own filter exists: `Tab` advances one screen and `BTab` goes back one from
  **each of the four screens with nothing focused** (8 cells), from **Wallet with the history filter
  open and `42ey` typed into it**, from **Send with the To field holding a 95-character address**,
  from **Send with Amount focused**, from **Send with the third field focused**, from **Log with the
  search open**, from **Node with the picker filter open**, with **the `?` overlay up**, and with
  **the §2n.5 privacy notice in flight**. 26 cells, 0 failures. A filter left behind survives the
  round trip — `42ey` was still in the box after `Tab` out and `⇧Tab` back, which is §7a.1's "commit,
  do not discard". And the **create-wallet modal consumes `Tab`** and renders, in the modal, the two
  lines it is required to render: `↑↓ fields · Enter next, then create · Esc discards` and
  `Tab is held here — Esc to discard first`. (Note for the next driver: tmux's name for Shift+Tab is
  `BTab`; `S-Tab` is delivered as a plain `Tab` and makes every backward case pass forwards.)

  **The mouse (complaint 2) — works, except DEFECT A.** Driven by writing real SGR-1006 reports to
  the pane (`ESC [ < 0 ; x ; y M` then `m`), which is byte-for-byte what a terminal sends, so this
  answers the implementer's own open question — *"the mouse has never been tried on the owner's own
  terminal … nothing here should be read as knowing it"*. It is now known: **this emulator's mouse
  reports reach the app and are routed.** Proved: a click on a picker row selects it and a **second
  click on the selected row activates it** and opened the confirm modal (`MouseResult::Activated`,
  no double-click interval); a click on the `[f]` chip fired the fetch path and brought up the
  privacy notice; a click on the Amount row focused Amount and a click on the Note row focused Note
  **without clobbering** what Amount held; a click on a priority chip changed the selection; the
  **wheel** scrolled the picker list and did **nothing** over the tab strip; a click anywhere closed
  the `?` overlay; **right-click closed it too**, as `Esc`. At the Narrow tier all four tab digits
  are clickable. Only the Wide/Medium tab strip is misaligned.

  **The picker (complaint 3) — works.** Entry renders the table from `nodes::cached` with no fetch:
  153 rows, the configured node first with `●`, the two column groups, and the footer
  `153 nodes · list 14m 53s ago · 37 skipped (no port) · 0 bad · source monero.fail`. Revision 4's
  two guards both hold on a real pty: `Enter` on entry refuses with `select a node first (↑↓ or
  click), then Enter`, and `Enter` on the configured row refuses with **`this is the node you are
  using`** and opens no modal. `Enter` on another row opened the confirm modal with the full address,
  `untrusted — a public node is never trusted (it would receive this wallet's own unspent amounts
  inside a request)`, the two applicable consequences (the third is conditional on an open wallet,
  and none was open), and `y to confirm · Esc cancels`. **On `y` the switch really ran**, through the
  same core sequence the CLI uses, inside the sandbox:

  ```
  before:  --daemon-address xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081     node.remote = xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  after:   --daemon-address 109.192.204.136:18081             node.remote = 109.192.204.136:18081
  wallet unit sha256(16): 5120e5861a1a37f2 -> 926c58c3f9dd5b78
  ```

  which closes the implementer's "the picker's confirm modal has never been run against a real

  **The wallet flow (complaint 4) — works, against a real `monero-wallet-rpc`.** `/usr/bin/monero-wallet-rpc`
  0.18.5.1 run by hand on scratch port 18583, cwd the sandbox's `state/run` so its own
  `monero-wallet-rpc.18583.login` lands where fmonero reads it, `--wallet-dir` in the sandbox,
  `--daemon-address xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081 --untrusted-daemon`. With it answering,
  `status --json` reported `node.reachable: null` in remote mode (§2j — never a number it was not
  given) and warnings `remote_node` + `wallet_closed`, both **info**. The empty state rendered

  `node: remote · xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081 · untrusted` — with the count real (it read
  `3 wallets` later in the same session). Then, from the keyboard alone:

  ```
  c -> name "reftiming" (a name that exists) -> refused INLINE in the modal:
       "reftiming already exists in wallet.dir"          nothing sent
  c -> name "tuimade", password, again, ↑↓ between all four controls, both password
       fields masked with ••, Enter on the last control
    -> the seed-backup modal, carrying core's own seed_backup_hint verbatim:
         "Back up your seed BEFORE funding this wallet:"

         "  # then type: seed"
         "fmonero never displays a seed — a scrollback is not a safe place for one."
         "remote mode: the node decided this wallet's restore height — keep it answering
          until the first refresh finishes."          <- §2m.3, in the only place it fits
    -> Enter -> the wallet is OPEN: "0.0000 XMR   unlocked 0.0000 XMR   tuimade",

    -> x -> back to the empty state, "wallet closed"
  ```

  So **a wallet has now been created through the TUI, against a real wallet daemon and a real public
  node** — which the implementer listed as never done. The seed was never displayed and never
  reached this log. The throwaway password appears in no file the run wrote (grepped). All three
  scratch wallets (`scratchver`, `reftiming`, `tuimade`) and the hand-run daemon are gone; the
  owner's own `wallet.dir` is still empty.

  **`[W]`, all three states.** Unit active and nothing open → `the wallet daemon is stopped`, the
  systemctl fixture flipped to `inactive`, the panel line became `idle · starts when you open a
  wallet`. Unit inactive → `⠙ starting the wallet daemon 1s / 30s · Esc cancels`, panel line
  `starting…`, fixture flipped to `active`. **A wallet open → refused**: `a wallet is open — press x
  to close it first`, fixture untouched. Exactly §7a.5.

  **One live run against monero.fail, from the sandbox, as sanctioned** (13.3 s wall):

  Eight probed, eight answered, one height between them — and the figure no third party could have
  supplied: **the node the owner is configured on was the slowest of the eight, at 3 344 ms against

  §2n.2's argument arriving as data rather than as prose, and it is worth the owner's attention
  independently of any defect here. `total` moved 152 → 153 within a day, which §2n.1 predicts.
  `--json` printed no disclosure on stderr, as §2n.5 rule 2 requires.

    columns will be empty until he presses `p`.

    is session-only state — but the one-key undo **after** a switch was not exercised.

- 2026-09-18 · independent verifier · **addendum to the verification above: DEFECT F, which the
  installer run found in the doctor rather than in the TUI.** Found after the entry above was
  committed, by running `fmonero doctor` on the owner's real configuration as the last act of the
  session. It had been **green** before this pass and was **red** after it, and the cause was the
  `scripts/install.sh --yes` the brief asked for:

  ```
  $ fmonero doctor --json | jq '[.checks[]|select(.status=="fail")]'
  [fail] state_perms: /home/oliver/.local/state/fmonero/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc.20260918T170624Z
                      is 0644, want 0600      || hint: null
  $ fmonero doctor --fix --dry-run --json
  {"ok":false,"planned":[],"checks":["state_perms"]}
  ```

  **Three faults in one line of `setup.rs`.** `setup --menu` backs the Omarchy menu file up with
  `std::fs::copy(&path, &backup)` (`crates/fmonero/src/setup.rs:487-490`), and `fs::copy`
  **carries the source file's mode to the destination**. The source is
  `~/.config/omarchy/extensions/omarchy-menu.jsonc`, which is **0644** — it is Omarchy's file, not
  fmonero's — so the copy lands at 0644 inside fmonero's own state directory, where §2b.7 says
  everything fmonero writes under `$HOME` is 0600 and where the doctor's `state_perms` check
  enforces exactly that. Then: · the check **fails**, · `doctor --fix` plans **nothing** for it, and
  · the check's `hint` is **`null`** — so the user is told they are broken, offered no repair, and
  given no command, which is the one thing §4 and §9's "One Rule" exist to prevent. `install.sh`'s
  own `umask 077` does not help: `fs::copy` calls `set_permissions` from the source metadata after
  creating the file.

  Reproduced from nothing, on a fresh sandbox with a 0644 menu file, which is the ordinary state of
  that file on any machine Omarchy has written it:

  ```
  source menu file mode: 644
  $ fmonero setup --menu --yes            # "6 Omarchy menu entries (backup: …)"
  644  <sandbox>/state/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc.20260918T171657Z
  $ fmonero doctor --fix --dry-run --json
  {"planned":[],"fails":["node_reachable","state_perms"]}
  ```

  So this is not a consequence of anything unusual about the owner's machine: any `setup --menu`
  that backs up an 0644 menu file leaves the doctor red and unfixable. The reason it had not fired
  before is that the 2026-09-17 backup was taken when the menu file was still the 0600 one fmonero
  had created; once Omarchy or the owner rewrote it at 0644, the next backup inherited that.
  The fix is one `set_permissions(&backup, 0o600)` after the copy, and — separately — a
  `state_perms` repair in `doctor --fix` with a hint, because a check that cannot be fixed and does
  not say how is worse than no check.

  **The damage this pass did was repaired, and it is the only thing this session changed on the
  owner's machine beyond the installer's one menu line:** `chmod 600` on that single backup file —
  the mode fmonero itself requires, on a file fmonero itself wrote, in fmonero's own state dir.
  `fmonero doctor` now exits **0** again with one warning, `wallet_rpc_reachable`, which is correct
  on a machine whose wallet daemon is deliberately down until a wallet is opened:

  ```
  $ fmonero doctor ; echo $?
  0
  warned: ["wallet_rpc_reachable"]
  ```

  Units still inactive and disabled with `NRestarts=0`, all four sha256 sums unchanged,
  `node.mode = remote`, `wallet.dir` empty.

  **One observation that is not a claim.** Two `fmonero-mock` processes (`--static … --port 18099`
  and `--role node --port 18100`) were found still listening at the end of the session, with logs
  under this session's scratchpad. `tests/e2e.sh --fast` and `--full` were each re-run afterwards
  and **neither leaked anything**, so this did not reproduce and is recorded as an observation
  rather than a defect. They were killed; nothing of this session is left listening.

  ### DEFECT A — the tab strip's hit rects (the blocker)

  **The fix is arithmetic.** `tab_strip` (`crates/fmonero/src/tui/screens.rs:195-212`) now advances
  by `label.width() + 2`, which is what `Tabs` draws: `tab_label` wraps every tab in one leading and
  one trailing cell (`ftui-widgets-0.7.0/src/tabs.rs:239-256`), and `.separator("")` costs nothing
  between them (`:474-479`). The old loop's comment — "the rects are computed from the same source
  as the text" — is gone, because it was the bug stated as a reassurance: the literals are the
  source of the *title*, not of the drawn width. Two smaller honesties came with it: the rect is
  clipped to the pane rather than dropped (`draw_text_span` clips too, so a clipped tab is still
  drawn and must still be clickable), and the comment records that the four labels total 41 columns
  against this tier's 60-column floor, so `Tabs`' overflow markers cannot fire here.

  **The test, and how the failure was proved.** `tui::tests::a_click_on_a_tab_label_lands_on_that_
  tab_and_never_on_its_neighbour` takes its coordinates **from the rendered frame** — it finds each
  title in row 0, clicks the title's last cell and the next tab's first cell — and starts each cell
  of the matrix **two screens away** from the tab under test, so neither the target nor the
  neighbour a misaligned rect selects is where the model already is and "nothing happened" cannot
  pass. Reverting the `+ 2` and nothing else:

  ```
  $ cargo test -p fmonero tui::tests::a_click_on_a_tab_label
  ---- a_click_on_a_tab_label_lands_on_that_tab_and_never_on_its_neighbour stdout ----
  panicked at crates/fmonero/src/tui/tests.rs:1540:13:
  assertion `left == right` failed: a click on column 19, the LAST cell of `2 Wallet` at 150x40
    (from Log), in the strip "  1 Node    2 Wallet    3 Send  [ 4 Log ]  …  omarchy"
    left: Send
   right: Wallet
  test result: FAILED. 0 passed; 1 failed
  ```

  That is the verifier's measurement reproduced by a test at the same geometry, which is the point:
  the suite was green through all of it because `find()` read the coordinate out of `m.hits` — the
  same map it then clicked — so it could prove routing and never alignment. The new case is the one
  assertion §7a.2's premise needs, and it runs at 150×40, 100×30, 90×30 (Medium) and 56×24 (Narrow).

  **And on a real pty, the same nine clicks, before and after.** `tmux new-session -d -x 150 -y 40`
  in a sandbox carrying the owner's own cached node list, driving the release binary; each click is
  `ESC [ < 0 ; col ; 1 M` then the same with `m`, 0.9 s settle, and the answer is read back from
  `capture-pane` row 0 — the active tab is the one `Tabs` brackets. The strip as drawn:

  ```
  [ 1 Node ]  2 Wallet    3 Send    4 Log
  123456789012345678901234567890123456789012345    (1-based columns)
  ```

  ```
  BEFORE (the reverted binary)                       AFTER
  col  8 'e'  -> [1 Node]    ok                      col  8 'e'  -> [1 Node]    ok
  col 11 ' '  -> [2 Wallet]  ok                      col 11 ' '  -> [2 Wallet]  ok
  col 20 't'  -> [3 Send]    WRONG                   col 20 't'  -> [2 Wallet]  ok
  col 23 '['  -> [3 Send]    ok                      col 23 ' '  -> [3 Send]    ok
  col 30 'd'  -> [4 Log]     WRONG                   col 30 'd'  -> [3 Send]    ok
  col 33 '['  -> [4 Log]     ok                      col 33 ' '  -> [4 Log]     ok
  col 39 'g'  -> [4 Log]     ok*                     col 39 'g'  -> [4 Log]     ok
  col 41 ']'  -> [4 Log]     ok*                     col 41 ']'  -> [4 Log]     ok
  col  3 '1'  -> [1 Node]    ok                      col  3 '1'  -> [1 Node]    ok
  ```

  \* **stated because it is a weakness of this script and not a pass**: the pty cases run in
  sequence from wherever the previous one left, so a case whose *wrong* answer equals the screen
  already showing reads `ok` — which is exactly what happened to `4 Log` here, the case the verifier
  measured as "nothing at all" from a fresh frame. The `ProgramSimulator` case is the one that
  discriminates every cell, because it rebuilds the model two screens away each time. The pty run is
  what proves the fix on a real terminal with real mouse bytes; the test is what proves it can never
  regress.

  The Narrow tier, which was already correct and draws its own strip, still is — `56×24`, strip
  `1·2·3·4  Node`: columns 1, 3, 5, 7 select Node, Wallet, Send, Log in order, and `q` quits
  cleanly.

  ### DEFECT B — the picker's address budget

  Two corrections in `picker_pane` (`screens.rs:590-613`), both of them arithmetic:

  · a kept column costs **`COL + 1`**, not `COL`, because `Table`'s `column_spacing` defaults to 1
    and becomes the `Flex` gap (`ftui-widgets-0.7.0/src/table.rs:114`, `:845`). The old loop stopped
    "with 26 left for the address" and actually left `26 - (n-1)`.
  · the minimum is **measured**: the widest address cell the table will draw (marker, address and
    `web`/`own` tag), capped at half the table's width so one 49-column hostname cannot delete every
    data column, and never below the **configured** row's own width. 26 was below the 31 cells
    `● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081` needs, so even correct arithmetic would have elided the
    address this machine is configured with.

  **The failure, proved by reverting the budget to `ADDRESS_MIN + COL * (n-1)` with `ADDRESS_MIN`
  26** — the new test then renders the defect at the first width it tries:

  ```
  $ cargo test -p fmonero tui::tests::the_picker_never_elides
  panicked at crates/fmonero/src/tui/tests.rs:1598:9: the configured address is elided at 90 columns:
  │ address              │health      │height      │ms          │node height │synced       │
  │ ● xmr-node.cakewallet│50% of 18   │3765000     │—           │—           │—            │
  test result: FAILED. 0 passed; 1 failed
  ```

  Post-fix, the same model rendered at seven widths — the configured address complete at every one,
  and the columns dropping from the right as the pane narrows, which is §7a.4's rule:

  ```
  width  90  address(48) health height ms                              ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 100  address(33) health height                                 ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 120  address(45) health height                                 ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 150  address(51) health height checked                         ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 180  address(57) health height checked ms                      ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 200  address(56) health height checked ms node-height          ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  width 240  address(68) health height checked ms node-height synced   ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081
  ```

  The address column is **wider at 90 columns than at 100** and that is the tier layout, not the
  budget: at 90 the terminal is Medium and the picker is the full width of the screen, while from
  100 it is Wide and the picker takes 62 % of it beside the Warnings pane (§7a.3). Inside one pane
  width the budget is monotonic.

  The test seeds the list with the owner's own address **and** the longest one monero.fail actually
  served this machine (`static.63.34.245.188.clients.your-server.de:18089`, 49 columns), and asserts
  at 180 that the long one is complete too — so the cap is exercised rather than assumed. Below that
  the cap does bite: a 51-column cell in a 60-column pane would leave nothing for health, and the
  half-pane rule prefers three honest columns to one. The configured row is the floor, always.

  **On the real pty**, at the brief's own 150×40, with the owner's cached list and his configured
  node, which is the capture the verification asked for:

  ```
  ╭ Public nodes ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
  │ address                                           │health      │height      │checked      │
  │ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081                   │95% of 19   │3765137     │5h 29m ago   │
  │   109.192.204.136:18081                           │100% of 18  │3765039     │9h 17m ago   │
  │   109.196.118.68:18081                            │100% of 18  │3765113     │6h 17m ago   │
  ```

  and the same TUI resized live to 90 and 180 columns, still complete:

  ```
  │ address                                        │health      │height      │ms           │
  │ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081                │95% of 19   │3765137     │273          │
  -- 180 columns
  │ address                                                 │health      │height      │checked     │ms           │
  │ ● xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081                         │95% of 19   │3765137     │5h 29m ago  │273          │
  ```

  **The `273` in that `ms` column is a real network contact and is disclosed here rather than
  buried**: entering the Node screen probes **the configured node only**, on §2n.4's schedule, and
  it did — one request to `xmr-node.cakewallet.com:18081`, the node the owner's own wallet already
  talks to, 273 ms, written into the **sandbox's** copy of the cache. monero.fail was not contacted
  at all in this round: the list was copied in from `~/.cache/fmonero/public-nodes.json` read-only
  and no fetch ran.

  ### DEFECT C — the `?` overlay's first row

  The row is now two rows, because one row could not state the rule truthfully:

  ```
  Tab  ⇧Tab  Ctrl+C   screens · quit — always, in a field, a modal, the overlay or a running
                      action (quit twice while one runs: cancel, then quit)
  1 2 3 4             the same two — but only when no field has focus: a field keeps every
                      printable key, and Esc unfocuses it
  q  Ctrl+C  …        (folded into the two rows above)
  ```

  `Ctrl+C` moved **into** the `always` row because it belongs there — `on_key` resolves it before
  `Tab` and before every branch (`mod.rs:1212-1215`) — and `1 2 3 4` and `q` moved out because they
  are printable characters and §7a.1's own second bullet gives a focused field every printable
  character. DESIGN.md §7a.1 and §7a.6 are amended to match (v5.5.1), including the sentence that
  specified the old row: the *behaviour* was right in v5.5 and the *specification of the text* was
  what was wrong, which is why this is a text change and not a behaviour change.

  **The test is the row and the dispatcher in one case**, which is what the brief meant by the
  KEYS-vs-dispatcher test: exactly one `KEYS` row may contain the word `always`, its key list must
  be exactly the keys resolved above every branch, the `1 2 3 4` row must carry its condition, the
  rendered overlay must no longer contain `even inside a field`, and then the behaviour on the money
  screen with real digits:

  ```
  Send screen, Amount focused, "0.01" typed, then 2 and 4  -> amount "0.0124", screen still Send
  then q                                                   -> amount "0.0124q" (typed, not obeyed)
  then Esc, then 2                                         -> screen Wallet   (the direct route)
  Send screen, "9" typed, then Tab                         -> screen Log      (`always`, in a field)
  Send screen, "9" typed, then Ctrl+C                      -> exit code 130   (`always`, in a field)
  ```

  With the old two rows restored the case fails on the first assertion:

  ```
  panicked at crates/fmonero/src/tui/tests.rs:1636:5: `always` may name only the keys resolved
  above every screen, focus state and modal
    left: "Tab  ⇧Tab  1 2 3 4"
   right: "Tab  ⇧Tab  Ctrl+C"
  ```

  ### FINDING D — the wallet badge

  `wallet_badge` (`mod.rs:591-616`) gains one state with two words. `starting` when one of the TUI's
  own actions is in flight and the unit is **not** active; `busy` when it is active and the poll
  still did not answer; `down` only when nothing of ours is running and the poll fails — which is
  the state the word describes. The predicate is `Action::wallet_busy()`
  (`subs.rs:184-195`), written as the **complement** of the four node-only actions (node up, node
  down, the list fetch, the probe sweep) so that a wallet action added later is busy by default and
  this badge's honesty does not depend on the list being extended. `NodeUseRemote` is therefore
  included on purpose: §2m.5 restarts the wallet unit, and the poll that fails during it is ours.

  **The brief asked for `down` "only when the unit is active, no action is in flight, and the poll
  still fails", and the unit-active clause is deliberately not written as a fourth condition**: core
  already splits the inactive case into `wallet_idle` (remote mode, no wallet — the daemon is
  *designed* to be down, §5, an `info`) and `wallet_down` (a warning: it should be running and is
  not), and the badge reads the first as `idle` two arms above. Adding `!unit_active => starting`
  would print `starting` for a daemon that died under an open wallet, with nothing starting it. The
  state the clause was protecting is exactly the state the new arm covers.

  **The test drives both unit states with and without an action**, through the §2i systemctl fixture
  so `unit_active` is a fact and not a guess, and asserts the rendered status line too:

  ```
  unit inactive, nothing in flight               -> "down"       (unchanged)
  unit active,   nothing in flight               -> "down"       (unchanged)
  unit inactive, Action::WalletDaemonStart armed -> "starting"    <- was "down"
  unit active,   Action::WalletCreate armed      -> "busy"        <- was "down"
  ```

  and it first asserts that the fixture really reaches the unit state it claims and that
  `wallet_idle` is **not** up, so it cannot pass through the arm above. With the new arm deleted:

  ```
  panicked at crates/fmonero/src/tui/tests.rs:1739:9: assertion failed: unit_active = false
    left: "down"
   right: "starting"
  ```

  ### FINDING E — the `Node` → `Nodes` label, decided: **left alone, and pinned by a test**

  The smaller honest option is not to build the refresh. Stated fully, because the brief asked for a
  decision and not a preference:

  · **What it would cost.** A table of every line fmonero has written for an id in an earlier
    version, compared byte-for-byte (modulo the trailing comma the merge itself adds) before a
    rewrite. It is sound, and it is a **permanent obligation**: one row per rendering change for
    ever, silently doing nothing the first time someone forgets — the "compiles, runs, does nothing"
    shape this document exists to prevent — and it weakens §4.2's one-sentence rule protecting a
    file the user maintains by hand, for a **label**.
  · **What leaving it costs.** The owner's `monero.node` entry keeps the label `Node`. Nothing
    functional depends on it: the id, the action and the screen it opens are unchanged, and that
    screen is the picker either way. The recovery is two commands he can read — delete the line from
    `~/.config/omarchy/extensions/omarchy-menu.jsonc`, then `fmonero setup --menu --yes`.
  · **Nothing shipped had to be retracted**, which is what made the choice easy: no README, `--help`
    or `robot-docs` line claims the rename reaches an installed machine (the claim lives in this
    file, in the 2026-09-18 implementer entry, and this paragraph is its correction). DESIGN.md §4.2
    now states the consequence and the recovery in the section that describes the merge — and its
    template listing, which still said `"label":"Node"` while the asset has said `Nodes` since
    v5.5, is corrected: the document and the asset disagreed about the very label this finding is
    about.
  · **A test pins it**: `setup::tests::an_existing_entry_keeps_its_own_label_even_when_the_asset_
    renames_it` writes the exact line the previous version wrote, runs the real `menu_step`, and
    asserts the line is byte-for-byte unchanged, that `"label":"Nodes"` did **not** appear, and that
    the five absent ids were still inserted. It passes on the pre-fix tree too, and says so: it
    records a decision rather than closing a defect.

  ### DEFECT F — the 0644 backup, and two claims in its report that do not reproduce

  **The defect is real and is fixed twice over.** `setup --menu`'s backup no longer goes through
  `std::fs::copy`, which calls `set_permissions` from the **source's** metadata after creating the
  destination — the source being Omarchy's own 0644 file. It is read and written through
  `fmonero_core::write_private`, the one 0600 create path (`O_EXCL` + `mode(0o600)`, write, fsync,
  rename). And the same three lines had the **directory** half of the fault: `create_dir_all` uses
  `0777 & ~umask`, so `state/backups/` was 0700 only because `install.sh` sets `umask 077` — a
  hand-run `fmonero setup --menu` under the ordinary 022 made it **0755**, which the doctor also
  fails. It now goes through a new `paths::ensure_private_dir`, which is `ensure_dirs`' own
  DirBuilder-at-0700 for one path; `ensure_dirs` calls it too, so there is one implementation.

  ```
  BEFORE (5004b95), umask 022, a 0644 source menu file
  $ fmonero setup --menu --yes          # "6 Omarchy menu entries (backup: …)"
  644 <sandbox>/state/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc.20260918T173309Z
  755 <sandbox>/state/backups
  $ fmonero doctor --only security --json | …
  state_perms: fail | …omarchy-menu.jsonc.20260918T173309Z is 0644, want 0600   exit 11

  AFTER, same umask, same 0644 source
  $ fmonero setup --menu --yes
  700 <sandbox>/state
  700 <sandbox>/state/backups
  600 <sandbox>/state/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc.20260918T180825Z
  $ fmonero doctor --only security --json | …
  state_perms: pass | 3 files and directories under the state, config and cache directories
                      are owner-only                                            exit 0
  ```

  The test asserts the mode of the backup **and** of the directory **and** that the backup is the
  bytes the merge was computed from; on the pre-fix code it fails with `left: 420  right: 384`
  (0644 against 0600).

  **The audit the brief asked for.** `std::fs::copy` appears exactly twice in the tree and only once
  in shipped code — the line above. The other is `doctor/fixture_tests.rs:94`, inside a `#[cfg(test)]`
  module, copying fixture inputs into a temp directory that is not under any fmonero root. The
  doctor's own backups already go through `write_private` (`mutate.rs:475`) and its run directories
  through `mkdir_private`, so nothing else in the tree carried a foreign mode into fmonero's state.
  Two neighbouring calls were checked and are correct as they are: `setup --menu`'s
  `fs::write` of the **Omarchy** file (not ours — and `write` on an existing file leaves its mode
  alone), and `units.rs:1146`'s `fs::write` of the §2i systemctl **fixture**, which is a test seam
  outside the state dir. §2b.7 now names both `fs::copy` and `create_dir_all` as forbidden under the
  three roots, with the reason, because the next implementer will reach for them again.

  **Two secondary claims in the addendum do not reproduce, and the correction is the point of
  running it rather than reading it.** The addendum says the doctor "plans **nothing** for it" and
  that "the check's `hint` is **`null`** — so the user is told they are broken, offered no repair,
  and given no command". On this tree, at `5004b95`, with a 0644 backup as the only perms failure:

  ```
  $ fmonero doctor --only security --json | jq -c '.checks[]|select(.id=="state_perms")|
      {status, fixable, class, fix_cmd}'
  {"status":"fail","fixable":true,"class":"A","fix_cmd":"fmonero doctor --fix"}

  $ fmonero doctor --only security            # on a pty
  fail:bad_mode   state_perms   <sandbox>/state/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc… is 0644, want 0600
                                fix: fmonero doctor --fix

  $ fmonero doctor --fix --dry-run --json | jq -r '.checks[]|select(.id=="state_perms")|
      .actions[].action'
  run chmod 600 <sandbox>/state/backups/omarchy-menu.jsonc…   (undo: chmod 644 <same file>)

  $ fmonero doctor --fix --only security --json | jq -c .summary
  {… "fixed":1, "fix_failed":0, "exit_code":0}      # the file went 0644 -> 0600
  ```

  So `state_perms` **is** class A, fixable, and repaired through the `mutate.rs` chokepoint with a
  recorded inverse — which is what the brief's "make it fixable … or explain why not" asks for, and
  the answer is that it already was. The `hint: null` half is true of the field read and false of
  the conclusion: a check row has **no** `hint` key (its keys are `actions, class, detail, fix_cmd,
  fix_error, fix_outcome, fixable, group, id, reason, severity, status, verdict`), and the command
  lives in `fix_cmd`, which was populated, and which the human renderer prints as `fix: fmonero
  doctor --fix` on the line under the failure. The lesson is the one this file keeps relearning from
  the other side: a `jq` path that does not exist returns `null`, and `null` read as "absent" turns
  a schema mistake into a defect report. The **only** real fault was the mode, and it is fixed.

  ### Documentation

  **README needed nothing removed for DEFECT A.** The brief expected a tab-strip caveat in README's
  Status section; there is none — the verifier put that finding in this file and in its commit
  message, and README's only tab-strip sentence is "Click a tab…" in the TUI section, which the fix
  makes true rather than requiring its removal. What README *did* under-claim is the round itself:
  its LIVE paragraph stopped at 2026-09-17, so the 2026-09-18 facts — a wallet created through the
  TUI against a real `monero-wallet-rpc` and a real public node, `node list --refresh --probe`
  against monero.fail, the picker's switch re-rendering real units, and the mouse driven with real
  SGR-1006 bytes on a real pty — were nowhere in it. One paragraph was added, and nothing in it is
  this round's own work. `BUILD_NOTE` was read and **not** changed, for the reason the verifier gave:
  it is stale but every sentence it makes is true and it under-claims, which is the safe direction.
  DESIGN.md carries six **v5.5.1** amendments: §2b.7 (the two forbidden calls), §4.2 (the backup's
  create path, the label decision, the corrected template line), §7a.1 and §7a.6 (the two KEYS
  rows), §7a.2 (the tab rects come from `Tabs`' geometry, and the frame-not-map test), §7a.4 (the
  column budget) and §7a's status-line paragraph (the badge's five words, in order).

  ### The owner's machine

  Untouched, and measured before and after the whole session:

  ```
  === units, before and after (identical) ===
  fmonero-monerod.service:    ActiveState=inactive UnitFileState=disabled NRestarts=0
  fmonero-wallet-rpc.service: ActiveState=inactive UnitFileState=disabled NRestarts=0
  === sha256, before and after (identical) ===
  ```

  -d`, or to this repository. No `sudo`, and no `systemctl` against his manager beyond the read-only
  `show -p ActiveState -p UnitFileState -p NRestarts` printed above.

  **One file of his does have a fresh mtime, and it is not this session's doing**:
  `~/.local/state/fmonero/state.json`, 20:08:54. It is the live bar widget — `quickshell -n -p
  /usr/share/omarchy/shell` is running, `Service.qml` polls `fmonero status --json` every 3 s, and
  `status` rewrites that document through §5's `with_state` chokepoint on every call. Its contents
  are the documented empty state (`last_open`, `last_error`, `net_sample`, `cold`, `wallet_behind`,
  `last_refresh` and `remote_probe` all `null`, `stalls` `{}`), so his configured node still has not
  been probed from his own configuration, exactly as the verification above left it.

  ### What this round does not claim

  - **A real send or sweep of any amount is still RED**, in every mode. No transaction was built and
    nothing was relayed; the Send screen was exercised on a simulator and, for DEFECT C, with four
    digits typed into an Amount field.
  - The mouse is proved on **this** emulator (tmux, SGR-1006) and, for the alignment, by arithmetic
    that will hold anywhere. Nothing here was tried under Hyprland's own terminal.
  - The `starting`/`busy` badge was proved with the §2i fixture and armed actions, **not** against a
    real `monero-wallet-rpc` coming up: the state it describes lasts a second or two on a real
    machine, and the verification above is where it was seen live (reading `down`).
  - `FINDING E` is a **decision**, not a fix: the owner's menu entry still says `Node`, and it will
    until he removes that line and re-runs `fmonero setup --menu --yes`.
  - The picker's cap means a **49-column** address can still be elided in a narrow pane; the
    configured row cannot, at any width the picker renders in. §7a.4 now says which of those two
    "never elided" means.

## Plain-language audit and the v5.6 design (real output)

- 2026-09-18 · audit + design · **## Plain-language audit and the v5.6 design (real output)** — the
  owner's three complaints, read against the program as it actually renders, turned into a contract
  (`DESIGN.md` §2o), a widget redesign (§6), a remote-mode Node screen built from what is known
  (§7a.3), a specification for the stale-node defect he reported (§7a.7) and a rewrite table with one
  row per string (`docs/COPY.md`). Baseline `52746ba`. **This round wrote no code.** Nothing in
  `crates/`, `plugin/` or `scripts/` was touched; DESIGN.md went from v5.5 to **v5.6**, DECISIONS.md
  gained this entry, and `docs/COPY.md` is new.

  ### The complaints, verbatim in substance

  - *"The text in the widget isn't clear or useful."*
  - *"Most of the text in the TUI is slop too. The explanations need to be in plain English."*
  - *"When I change nodes it says I'm still on Cake Wallet."*
  - *"I don't want to manage the CLI; that's what the TUI is for."*
  - *"Like fbittorrent, the window is closed, the widget continues. Same thing here please."*

  All five are accurate about `52746ba`. The first two are one defect with 450 instances, the third is
  a cached `Context`, the fourth is a rule the program did not have, and the fifth is behaviour the
  program already had and never mentioned.

  ### What was captured, on this machine, in a sandbox

  Every string below was read off a real run of the installed-equivalent binaries
  (`target/release/fmonero`, `target/release/fmonero-mock`, both built at `52746ba`), under
  `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` / `FMONERO_CONFIG_DIR` / `FMONERO_CACHE_DIR` / `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR`
  pointing into a scratch directory. **The owner's `~/.config/fmonero`, his wallet, his units and his
  `~/.local/state/fmonero` were not read or written**, no `sudo` ran, and nothing was pushed.

  - `fmonero status`, `doctor`, `node info`, `node peers`, `node list`, `settings list`, `wallet list`,
    `wallet balance`, `version`, `--help` and three `--help` subcommands, each in **both** human mode
    (through `script -qc`, so stdout is a pty) and `--json` (non-TTY, which §4 makes imply `--json`).
  - Eleven refusals, triggered rather than imagined: a 1-character address (exit 15), `all` as an
    amount (exit 2 `bad_amount`), `0.1.2` (exit 2), no wallet open (exit 4 `unreachable`), no node unit
    (exit 4 `unit_missing`), `node up` while a public node is configured (exit 4 `remote_node`),
    `node down --dry-run` (exit 2 `unsupported_flag`), an unknown settings key (exit 2 `unknown_key`),
    `wallet open` with no password on a non-TTY (exit 9 `bad_password`).
  - The TUI on a 120×40 tmux pty against `fmonero-mock`: all four screens, the public-node picker with
    and without a cache, the create-wallet modal with all four fields filled, the receive QR, the help
    overlay, and the Node screen in **public-node mode**.

  Two figures from those captures, because they size the problem: one `fmonero doctor` run printed
  `40 checks: 16 pass, 2 warn, 1 fail, 21 skip`, and **twelve of those twenty-one skips printed the
  same 28-word sentence** — *"node.mode is remote (…): there is no local monerod to ask, and no
  wallet-RPC method reports a remote daemon's own state (§1b.9 item 5)"*. The Node screen in
  public-node mode devoted two of its four panes to the words `not available with a remote node` and a
  third to *"fmonero does not talk to this node — monero-wallet-rpc does."*

  ### The count

  450 strings inventoried across 13 surfaces; **392 rows rewritten** (432 counted by literal, since
  nine rows bundle a set such as the seven picker column headers); 58 confirmed already plain and left
  alone; 4 deleted outright; 7 new. `docs/COPY.md` carries every one with its `file:line`, the current
  text, the new text and which rule the old text broke.

  ### The decisions, and what each one costs

  1. **§2o is a contract, not a style guide.** Twelve rules, a vocabulary table with one name per
     thing, and a `scripts/check.sh` step with an allow-list for command lines and a self-test that the
     banned words fire. The rule that does the most work is rule 5: an id, a reason or a `code_name` is
     `--json` only. `status --json`'s ids, reasons and keys do **not** change, so no agent contract
     moves.
  2. **§2o.1 — no command line in the TUI or the widget.** This is the owner's fourth complaint as a
     rule, and it forced two design changes rather than two rewrites. `node local` **enters the TUI**
     on `l` (§7a.4), because its only route was a printed command and deleting the route strands the
     user who switched to a public node from that very screen; and the `a` modal's footer stops
     advertising `--trusted`. Four shell commands survive in the TUI, each because fmonero has no code
     that could do the job: installing Monero, reading a seed (§2b forbids displaying one), restoring
     from a seed (§0 cuts it), and `wallet sweep` — the only one caused by a scope decision rather than
     an impossibility, and `docs/COPY.md` §2 says so and says the line goes when sweep arrives.
  3. **The widget's header line is at most five words and the panel shows only rows that carry
     information.** Five detail rows and two footer buttons go; a first-match table decides the header,
     ordered by what the user is waiting for rather than by what came first in the JSON. §6.5 carries a
     text mock of all six states. `Service.qml`'s budget **falls** from 420 to 340, because 420 was set
     for work that came in at 315 and unspent headroom is an invitation.
  4. **The Node screen shows what is known instead of what is not.** The cached check of §2n.4 already
     held a height, a sync flag, a latency and an age; the screen said five things were unavailable and
     never showed it. One pane, four facts, three states, and the height and its age always render
     together — a four-hour-old `synced` says nothing about now.
  5. **The stale-node defect is specified as three parts, not one** (§7a.7): reload the `Context` after
     any action that writes settings, notice a change made from another terminal by `stat`-ing
     `config.toml` on the 1 s poll, and drop the address-matched cached check so the new node does not
     read `Not checked yet.` for a minute after the user picked it from a row that had just been
     measured. The test is a `ProgramSimulator` case asserting **all four** of the surfaces that were
     wrong — hero, status bar, picker marker, facts pane — within one poll, because the defect lived in
     the gap between two functions that are each correct.
  6. **Closing the window was already safe and v5.6 says so.** Nothing in the runtime changes: `quit`
     never closes the wallet and the 20 s auto-refresh runs inside the wallet service. What changes is
     that the Wallet screen states `This keeps going in the background. You can close this window.`
     while a wallet is behind, and the widget's headline becomes `Wallet syncing 63% · 240 000 blocks
     left` with the icon ring drawing that progress. The widget's poll gains
     **`status --json --check-node`** (§4, §5) so a public node's height stops going stale with nobody
     to refresh it: identical output, spawns the bounded `curl` **detached** only when the configured
     node's cached check is older than `node.remote_probe_sec`, records the attempt so several polls
     spawn once, and is a no-op with your own node. Bare `status` still spawns nothing, which is what
     every §2l measurement rests on.
  7. **`Warning` gains one field, `action: Option<String>`** (§5, §5b.5). `hint` stays the CLI's
     runnable command; `action` is the in-app sentence the TUI and the widget render instead. `None`
     means nothing can be done from inside the app, and both surfaces then print the text alone rather
     than inventing a key. This is the mechanism §2o.1 needs: before it, those two surfaces had only
     `hint` to show, which is literally how `fmonero node local --yes` came to be printed on a bar.
  8. **The budgets moved before the code, which is §7a's own procedure.** `TUI_BUDGET` 7 200 → **7 600**
     against a measured 7 197 (three lines of headroom), itemised in §7a as six line items totalling
     ≈ 320, with the ceiling set at 7 600 rather than 7 550 because an estimate consuming 99.7 % of its
     own ceiling is what v5.5 revision 12 identified as the arithmetic that forces a dishonest trade.
     `QML_BUDGET` 1 516 → **1 529**, and this time the figure's derivation is written out: the four
     per-file budgets (340 + 820 + 112 + 225 = 1 497) plus `manifest.json`'s 32 real lines, which the
     gate counts.

  ### One observation for the implementer, found while capturing

  `fmonero node remote <addr> --yes` under an `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` sandbox exits **3
  `sandboxed_units`** — and it has already written `node.mode = "remote"` and `node.remote` to
  `config.toml` by then, because §2m.5 puts the single `Settings::save` before `units::install`. The
  sandbox's `FMONERO_SYSTEMD_USER_DIR` was set and still nothing was written to it: the guard is on
  `FMONERO_STATE_DIR`, by v5.3's deliberate decision. On a real machine this path cannot be reached, so
  it is not a defect — but **a test that asserts "a refused `node remote` changed nothing" would be
  asserting something false**, and the next round's own captures depend on knowing it (this session's
  public-node CLI captures were produced exactly that way, deliberately).

  ### What this round does not claim

  - **No code changed, so nothing here is verified by a test.** Every string in `docs/COPY.md`'s
    "becomes" column is a specification, not an observation. The implementer's gate is the one that
    proves them.
  - **A real send or sweep of any amount is still RED**, in every mode. Nothing was built and nothing
    was relayed; the Send screen was read, not driven.
  - The stale-node defect is **confirmed in code and in the captures' shape**, not reproduced live on
    the owner's own configuration: doing that would mean switching his node.
  - The `--check-node` flag is specified and unimplemented. Its four bounds each name a test in §5, and
    none of those tests exists yet.
  - `scripts/check.sh` still carries `TUI_BUDGET=7200` and `QML_BUDGET=1516`. §2o's grep, §6.7's three
    new assertions and both new budgets are specified here and land with the code.
  - The five-word header budget was measured against `Style.space(380)` and the bar's own font on this
    machine's config, not on an arbitrary bar width.

  ### The stale-node defect: what it was, and the three cases that hold it shut

  `Tui` held **one** `Context`, cloned at startup, and never reloaded it.
  `Action::NodeUseRemote` rewrote `config.toml` and re-rendered the wallet unit correctly — the
  switch really happened, and `fmonero status --json` from another terminal proved it — but
  `self.ctx.settings` kept the old `node.remote`, so every poll asked `status` about the old node,
  the Node hero printed it, the status bar printed it, and the picker's `●` stayed on the old row.
  The address was right in `config.toml`, right in the unit, right in the wallet service's argv,
  and wrong in every pixel. **That the defect survived a real-pty round is itself the lesson**:
  every capture started a fresh TUI, and a stale cache is invisible to a process that has just been
  born.

  ```
  test tui::tests::switching_nodes_moves_every_surface_within_one_poll ... ok
  test tui::tests::a_settings_change_from_outside_is_noticed_on_the_next_poll ... ok
  test tui::tests::an_unreadable_settings_file_keeps_the_old_values_and_says_so_once ... ok
  ```

  Each asserts **all four** surfaces that were wrong — the model's own `Context`, the hero, the
  status-bar segment and the picker's `●` — plus the fifth v5.6 adds: ` This node ` shows the new
  node's block rather than `Not checked yet.` The third case is the one worth keeping honest: an
  unreadable `config.toml` mid-session leaves the previous values on screen, raises **one** toast,
  and does not panic. It found a real ordering bug while being written — the poll armed §2n.4's
  node check in the same tick and its label overwrote the toast, so the failure was invisible. The
  poll now skips that tick's outbound work when a reload failed, which is both the fix and the
  honest behaviour: fmonero does not go checking nodes while it cannot read the settings that name
  them.

  ### `status --json --check-node`, and the four bounds that keep it from being a spawn loop

  §2e says `status` starts nothing, and that rule left a public node's block number and sync flag
  with **no refresher at all**: the widget polls `status`, `status` spawns nothing, the TUI is the
  only thing that ever checked, and `checked 4h ago` was therefore the normal state of the bar.
  The flag prints the identical document and, only when the cached check of the configured node is
  older than `node.remote_probe_sec`, starts §2n.4's bounded `curl` **detached** and returns at
  once. It does not make itself fresh; it makes the next call fresh.

  ```
  test status::tests::the_check_flag_starts_nothing_when_the_last_check_is_fresh ... ok
  test status::tests::a_stale_check_starts_one_and_three_polls_still_start_one ... ok
  test status::tests::with_your_own_node_the_check_flag_does_nothing ... ok
  test status::tests::plain_status_starts_nothing_even_with_a_stale_check ... ok
  test status::tests::a_harvested_answer_becomes_the_cached_check_and_a_silence_becomes_a_failure ... ok
  ```

  The last one is the half §5 had not specified: the detached child's stdout lands in
  `<state_dir>/remote-check.json` at 0600, and the next `--check-node` call harvests it — an answer
  becomes that address's cached check with the time it took, an **empty** file past the child's own
  deadline becomes a recorded failure (so the Node screen can say `No answer when checked 2m ago.`
  instead of `Not checked yet.` for ever), an empty file before it is left alone, and an answer
  about an address we have since left is discarded unread. §5 and §5b now say all of that.

  **And the test is not written the way §5 imagined it.** §5 asked for "a fake `curl` on `PATH`
  records zero invocations". `PATH` is process-global and `cargo test` runs its tests in threads, so
  a test that mutates it would decide the outcome of whichever other test was running. The bounds
  are asserted on what is recorded beside the cached result and on whether a spawn was attempted at
  all, which is the same property by a route that is safe in a threaded suite. DESIGN.md §5 records
  the substitution rather than leaving the next agent to find the paragraph untrue.

  ### §2o's grep, and the four strings it caught that 450 hand-written rows missed

  ```
  == §2o  no banned word in a user-facing string
    ok    §2o rule 7: no banned word in the TUI, output.rs, status.rs or the widget
    ok    self-test: §2o fires on a planted "the wallet daemon is not running"
    ok    self-test: §2o exempts "In a terminal: omarchy pkg add monero"
  ```

  It extracts the **string literals** of `tui/*.rs`, `output.rs`, `status.rs` and the widget —
  comments stripped, the file folded into one line first so a literal broken across lines with a
  trailing `\` is examined whole — cuts each Rust file at `#[cfg(test)]`, and exempts two shapes: a
  runnable command (rule 11's whole point) and a **single bare token**, because `"wallet_idle"` is
  a contract key and prose has spaces in it. On its first run it fired on four literals
  `docs/COPY.md` §3 had not named: `new subaddress {i}`, `daemon log level {n}`,
  `a wallet name may not contain `/` — the daemon refuses it`, and the modal title
  ` Label subaddress {i} `. That is the entire argument for having it.

  Two blind spots, in the step's own comment where whoever hits them will read it: `build` is not
  in the pattern, because the word is unavoidable in the code around the strings and a grep that
  fires on `fn build_transfer` is a grep the next agent relaxes; and a `//` **inside** a literal on
  a continuation line reads as the start of a comment.

  units are inactive — no case started one"*. The menu file is at
  `~/.config/omarchy/extensions/omarchy-menu.jsonc`, not the `menus/` path the round's own
  instructions guessed at, which is worth writing down because a hash of a file that does not exist
  is a check that passes for the wrong reason.

  ### Two defects in `tests/tui_tmux.sh --capture`, older than this round, found by running it

  Neither is a regression; both were invisible because the capture path is the one nobody runs.

  1. **The capture run moved between Send fields with `Tab`**, which v5.5 made a *screen* key in
     every focus state (§7a.1). The amount was therefore typed into the Log screen, no build ever
     happened, the confirm-modal assertion failed — and the `nothing was relayed` assertion **passed
     because nothing was built**, which is exactly the vacuous shape that block's own comment warns
     about. The same script asserts the new rule forty lines further down, so it had been
     contradicting itself since v5.5. It sends `Down` now.
  2. **The wallet empty-state block ran against an open wallet.** `fmonero-mock` is stateless by
     design: `x` really issues `close_wallet`, the fixture really answers, and `get_address` keeps
     answering too, so `status` goes on reporting an open wallet and that screen is unreachable
     under `--capture`. The close is asserted on what is observable (the toast) and the four needles
     are skipped with the reason — a test that fails on a correct program is a test that gets
     deleted.

  ### Deviations from the specification, each recorded where it belongs

  | What | Why | Where |
  |---|---|---|
  | the widget's locked-funds row omits the amount the TUI's identical line prints | it is `balance - unlocked`, arithmetic on money, which §2a.5 forbids in QML outright; Status carries no pre-rendered locked amount | DESIGN.md §6.5, `docs/COPY.md` §7 |
  | `headerLine` tests `layout === "none"` **before** `!nodeUp` | in §6.4's written order the `No node set up yet` row could never match, because a machine with no node also reports `reachable: false` | DESIGN.md §6.4 |
  | the seed modal does not print `Close this wallet first — press x.` | §2o rule 12 forbids the em-dash the illustration used, and core's own `With the wallet closed, in a terminal:` already says it | `docs/COPY.md` §7 |
  | the addresses pane is ` Addresses · … `, not ` Your addresses — … ` | the specified title is 49 columns in a 48-column pane and clipped to `… l renam` | `docs/COPY.md` §7 |
  | `monerod_conf_mode`'s doctor title changed, though §3.12 called it already plain | it named `rpc-login`, a flag, in prose | `docs/COPY.md` §7 |
  | the `--check-node` bounds are not tested with a fake `curl` on `PATH` | `PATH` is process-global and the suite threads | DESIGN.md §5 |

  ### What this round does not claim

  - **A real send or sweep of any amount is still RED**, in every mode. Nothing was built against a
    real daemon and nothing was relayed; the Send screen's captures come from `fmonero-mock`, and
    the confirm modal was reached and then discarded with `Esc`.
  - **The stale-node fix is proved against `fmonero-mock` and a real `config.toml`, not against the
    owner's own switch.** Reproducing it on his machine would mean changing the node he is using.
    The three cases drive the same `Msg` the real action produces and the same file the real
    `set_remote` writes; `node_mode::set_remote` itself is not called in a test, because under
    `FMONERO_STATE_DIR` it exits 3 `sandboxed_units` at `units::install` **after** having saved the
    settings (recorded 2026-09-18), so calling it would test the sandbox guard and not the fix.
  - **`--check-node` has never contacted a real public node from this round.** Its five tests use a
    loopback address nothing listens on and a planted answer file; the detached spawn is real, the
    answer is not.
  - **The widget's six rendered states are asserted through `Model.js` under `node`, not through
    Quickshell.** What a smoke test can see of the panel is its source and its formatters; the
    journal check proves it loads without error, and the six mock-ups in §6.5 are what the rows are
    written against.
  - **`§7a`'s line budget is now exactly 7 600 of 7 600.** That is inside the ceiling DESIGN.md
    raised before this code was written, and it is also the first thing the next agent will hit:
    one added line fails `scripts/check.sh`. The honest options then are to delete something or to
    raise the number in DESIGN.md first, in that order — not to widen the grep's file list.

## Plain-language round verification (real output)

- 2026-09-19 · independent verification · **## Plain-language round verification (real output)** —
  every gate re-run at `2670841`, then the two surfaces read as a non-technical Monero user on a
  real tmux pty at **120x32 and 150x40**, the widget's formatters driven against **real
  `status --json` documents**, and the stale-node fix reproduced live in both directions. All ten
  gates pass and every number the implementer reported is reproduced. **Eighteen findings follow,
  three of them serious**: the bar widget running on the owner's bar right now is the *pre-v5.6*
  `Panel.qml`; the installed binary was not HEAD before this run; and decision 7's own new lines
  render outside every frame on the Wallet screen. One README string was fixed (it named an amount
  the formatter cannot emit); no other code was changed.

  ### V1 — THE WIDGET ON THE OWNER'S BAR IS THE PRE-v5.6 PANEL, AND THE GATE CANNOT SEE IT

  `omarchy-shell oliver.monero cursor`, live, after `scripts/install.sh --yes` and after
  `omarchy-shell shell rescanPlugins`:

  ```
  {"section":"header","index":0,"active":false,"opened":false,
   "sections":["header","warnings","footer"],"alive":true,"binaryMissing":false,
   "nodeUp":false,"synced":false,"walletOpen":false,"remote":true,"iconCase":7,"iconColor":"#ffffff"}
  ```

  `grep -c '"warnings"' plugin/oliver.monero/Panel.qml` is **0**. The repo's `navSections` builds
  `header · problems · wallet · pending · footer`; `"warnings"` is the section name of the panel
  **before** `af73411`, which is also the last panel without v5.6's `walletBehind` icon case. So the
  shell is still running the v5.5 widget: the symlink points at the repo (`ls -l` confirms), but
  Quickshell holds the loaded QML, `rescanPlugins` did not replace it (§1.5 says symlinks do not
  hot-reload), and `omarchy restart shell` is **refused while the session is locked** — which it was
  for this whole run. Nothing is wrong with the code; what is wrong is that **the redesign that
  answers the owner's first complaint has never rendered on his bar**, and neither the
  implementer's gate nor mine could tell, because `tests/plugin-smoke.sh`'s live half asserts only
  that `cursor` returns a JSON object:

  ```
  ok    widget is loaded (cursor: {… "sections":["header","warnings","footer"] …})
  ```

  The evidence was inside the passing assertion's own message, twice, unread. The fix is one line in
  §6.7: assert the cursor's `sections` are the ones `Panel.qml` builds (or stamp a version token into
  the cursor payload), so a stale shell fails the gate instead of decorating it. The owner sees v5.6
  the first time he unlocks and the shell restarts; until then the bar is v5.5.

  ### V2 — `~/.local/bin/fmonero` was not HEAD before this run

  The release build on this machine is **reproducible**: `touch crates/fmonero/src/main.rs && cargo
  build --release` twice gives the same `sha256`. So the hashes are evidence.

  ```
  installed before this run   5a69ed0abc0e78224223c73cffd779c1e1b5b1fb7214bb891139e68caa64d953
  HEAD (2670841) builds to    0264f0cb2dba7b485046f24558360f8eb2dca9a2100a72c1988b7e11037f07ab
  HEAD~1's tui/* builds to    228ff0fa2177199ecef9c3c7874738b86e92ce977edc85c53a496bd80925e3cf
  installed after install.sh  0264f0cb…  = HEAD
  ```

  `2670841` changed `tui/mod.rs` and `tui/screens.rs` by 26 lines *after* the round's own install, so
  the owner has been running a binary older than the last two code commits. It is HEAD now.

  ### V3 — decision 7's own lines render outside every frame on the Wallet screen

  A public node whose block is ahead of the wallet's (mock node at 3 781 122, wallet at 3 541 122),
  120x32 and again at 150x40. The Node screen puts the two lines inside its ` Node ` pane, correctly.
  The Wallet screen draws them **at column 0, in the gap between two framed panes**:

  ```
  ╭ Balance ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
  │ 1.234567891234 XMR   unlocked 1.234567891234 XMR                                             │
  ╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
  Syncing 93% · 240 000 blocks left.
  Syncing in the background. You can close this window.
  ╭ Addresses · Enter copies · a new · l rename ───╮╭ Payments  6 · t: all ──────────────────────╮
  ```

  `screens.rs:1238` splits `rows[1]` and calls `lines_at(…, split[0], false, frame)` — a bare rect,
  no `framed()`, no padding — so the two lines the owner asked for are the only unframed,
  flush-left text in the program. The arithmetic behind them is right (3 541 122 of 3 781 122 is
  93 %, 240 000 left); the placement is not.

  ### V4 — `l`, the in-app route decision 6 added, opens a confirm that states nothing

  §7a.4 requires the `Use your own node` modal to state three things: the wallet service will
  restart · `{name}` will be closed, and offered again · and, when the node is not running,
  *"Your own node is not running yet. Switching will not start it."* What renders (120x32, a public
  node configured, `l` pressed):

  ```
  ┌─────────────────── Use your own node ────────────────────┐
  │                 y confirms · Esc cancels                 │
  │                                                          │
  │                    [ OK ]  [ Cancel ]                    │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  ```

  `mod.rs::confirm_local` builds exactly `"y confirms · Esc cancels"`, plus `"{name} will be closed."`
  when `status.wallet.name` is `Some`. The other confirm on the same screen — `Enter`, use this node —
  is complete and is the shape this one needs:

  ```
  ╭ Use this node ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮
  │ 127.0.0.1:18492                                                        │
  │ fmonero will not tell this node which amounts your wallet holds.       │
  │ · the wallet service will restart                                      │
  │ · this node will see your IP address and when you ask it anything      │
  │ y to confirm · Esc cancels                                             │
  ╰────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
  ```

  The same capture is in `decisions/`'s own `node-remote-local.txt` from the implementer's run, where
  the gate read it as *"l opens the confirm that acts"* and did not read what the confirm says.

  ### V5 — the Node screen's `this wallet` row prints a machine timestamp and an unspaced block

  With a wallet open and one real refresh recorded, 120x32:

  ```
  ╭ Node ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────╮╭ This node ───────────────────────╮
  │ ● Public node · 127.0.0.1:18591                            ││ block 3 541 122                  │
  │ this wallet block 3541122 · checked 2026-09-19T00:20:07+02  ││ synced                           │
  │                                                            ││ checked 2s ago                   │
  ```

  Three things in one line: an RFC3339 timestamp as prose, **clipped mid-offset**; a block number
  with no separators (§2o rule 9 asks for `block 3 541 122`, which the pane 60 columns to the right
  gets right); and the word `checked`, which the same screen uses for the node's own measurement two
  lines away. `screens.rs:760` formats `"block {} · checked {}"` from `opt(wallet.height)` and
  `wallet.last_refresh_at`, which §5 defines as an RFC3339 string. With no refresh recorded the row
  reads `checked —`.

  ### V8 — `monero-wallet-rpc is not answering: io: Connection refused (os error 111)` reaches all three surfaces

  Live, in the TUI's status bar (120x32, no wallet service running):

  ```
  ● public node node.example.com:18089 ● wallet closed │ close: monero-wallet-rpc is not answering: io: ⇥ screens  ? help
  ```

  and in the CLI:

  ```
  $ fmonero wallet refresh
  fmonero: error[backend]: monero-wallet-rpc is not answering: io: Connection refused (os error 111)
    reason: unreachable
    Try: fmonero wallet open <name>   # starts the unit on demand, then opens
  ```

  and it can reach the **bar panel**: `Service.qml:280` assigns a failed action's stderr to
  `localError` and `:29` takes `status.last_error.text`, both of which `Panel.qml` renders.
  One function is the source — `rpc/jsonrpc.rs:360` and `:372` pass the literal strings `"monerod"`
  and `"monero-wallet-rpc"` into `shared_error`, whose five arms then print
  `{daemon} is not answering: {msg}` · `{daemon} refused the credential fmonero has for it` ·
  `{daemon} did not answer {what} within its deadline` (with `{what}` an RPC method name) ·
  `{daemon} was killed at its deadline …` · `{daemon} does not serve {path}`. §2o.2's vocabulary
  table names this exact string as the thing never to say, and the hint's own comment says `unit`.
  `docs/COPY.md` §3.11 inventories thirty core error strings and **not this family**, which its own
  code comment calls *"the most frequently hit error path in the tool"*.

  ### V9 — `fmonero settings list` is an un-inventoried surface, section numbers and all

  ```
    node.mode               remote
      local | remote — which daemon monero-wallet-rpc is pointed at (§2m)  [default local]
    node.unit               fmonero-monerod.service
      the systemd user unit that runs monerod  [default fmonero-monerod.service]
    wallet.priority         2
      default fee priority 0-4 (2 = normal; 0 is an alias of the lowest tier, §1b.4)  [default 2]
    wallet.autostart        false
      start the wallet daemon when `fmonero tui` launches (TUI only, §7a.5)  [default false]
    timeouts.node_sec       3
      node RPC probe deadline  [default 3]
  ```

  `SETTINGS_KEYS` in `settings.rs:405-510` is 24 rows; seven carry a `§` number (rule 4 forbids them
  outright) and the set carries daemon · unit · systemd · RPC · loopback · probe · priority · LMDB ·
  `--prune-blockchain` · `--wallet-dir` · `open_wallet` · `trusted`. `docs/COPY.md` §6 lists the
  fourteen surfaces it inventoried and this is not one of them, and §4 does not exempt it. The
  `wallet.priority` row is the same *priority 0-4, 0 is an alias* register the Send screen was
  cleaned of in this round, still live one command away.

  ### V10 — the doctor's titles were rewritten; most of its details were not

  `docs/COPY.md` §3.12 rewrote all forty **titles** and then the **details that appeared in one live
  capture**. Running §2o's own extractor over `doctor/checks.rs` with `scripts/check.sh`'s own
  pattern gives **51 literals that would fire it**, among them:

  ```
  "{} and {} match units::render_* byte for byte"
  "the unit says --daemon-address {address} while the settings say {}: a monero-wallet-rpc started …"
  "{name} carries --log-level {level}: above 0 epee logs HTTP headers and wallet2 internals …"
  "the snapshot is format {} and this build writes {}: postcard is not self-describing …"
  "high water {}, {behind} behind the wallet's {wh}. The next `wallet history` … (§5b)"
  "a restricted node reports 0 connections by construction (§1b.3)"
  ```

  Nearly all of them are reachable **only on a machine whose node runs** — which is why the capture
  never saw them, and which is the same blind spot this round named twice about itself. One is
  visible today: `units_present` builds `the {unit} service is not set up yet`, so a person reads
  *"the fmonero-monerod.service service is not set up yet"* — a unit name as prose, and a stutter.
